/* ============================================================ PULSE Strategy — Cloudflare Worker Built 2026-09-02, per continuity brief §26 (confirmed real build scope). Auth model copied faithfully from PULSE Pricing's real, live Worker (read directly from Pulse_Pricing_Worker_v1m.md, not reinvented): PBKDF2-hashed per-user PINs, Bearer session tokens in D1 + KV (KV checked first, D1 fallback), forced-PIN-reset on first login via short-lived reset tokens, server-emailed one-time PINs via Resend, admin-only user management with the same safety guards (can't delete/ deactivate the last active admin, can't touch your own account, duplicate names blocked case-insensitively, sessions revoked on deactivate/demote, audit-friendly actor snapshots). Differences from Pricing, deliberate: - VALID_ROLES = ['admin'] only for now (brief §10: "for the foreseeable future we'll only be looking at Admins" — Strategy has no non-admin role yet, unlike Pricing's admin/sales split). - No "offers" concept, so deleteUser has no reassignOffersTo step — Strategy's records (Baseline budgets, weekly fact rows) are never owned by a single user the way Pricing's offers are, so there is nothing to reassign on delete. - Added Baseline routes (regions, revenue/sales budgets) — new to this segment, not present in Pricing at all. - Env bindings renamed to Strategy's own (separate Worker + D1 + KV, confirmed §26: "Strategy gets its own, separate Cloudflare Worker and its own D1 database"). Explicitly NOT included this pass (§26): proposal pipeline/volume routes, weekly-upload ingestion routes for bookings/backlog (tables exist in schema.sql; the upload endpoint itself is a follow-up once the real file shapes are locked with the actual admin-upload UI), historical benchmark load. Do not add without re-confirming scope. §55 Worker batch A (2026-09-04): monthly Revenue Budget entry/read against revenue_budgets_monthly, and Forecast's budget context (Month Pace, Regional Drivers) now prefers real monthly Revenue data where it exists, falling back to annual÷12 everywhere else. Sales Budgets stays annual-only by design (Misty's explicit call, 2026-09-04: "sales tables did not have monthly budgets... we could do the divide by 12 methodology for sales to monitor momentum") — there is no sales_budgets_monthly table, and Sales-side pace comparisons always use annual÷12, never a real monthly figure. Regional Drivers' Revenue figures (added same batch, per Misty's follow-up "add in Revenue regional drivers too") are budget context only, not a pace comparison — there is still no Revenue-actuals fact table in this schema (no `billed_fact` source), so each region comes back with `revenueActualsAvailable: false` and no pace percentage. Every region gets a real Revenue Budget figure; nothing here is illustrative or invented. §55 Worker batch B (2026-09-04): Merge Region (§55.3) and Service Alignment (§55.5) routes, both standalone workflows runnable anytime — NOT gated by the Setup Baseline wizard's annual lock/unlock cadence, unlike the wizard steps themselves (not yet built). Merge Region uses the now-live `region_merges` audit table and `regions.active` soft-delete flag: a merge is preview-then-confirm-then-atomic-execute, never a silent one-step action, and the source region is never hard- deleted (its historical rows stay attributable, `regions.active` just flips to 0 so it drops out of active-region pickers). Service Alignment uses the `service_alignment` table (natural key `(service_family, service_name, effective_fiscal_year)` — deliberately excludes `line_of_business`, since LOB is the value being assigned, not part of what makes a row unique, see schema.sql's comment on this table for the reasoning). The `asOf`-style "as reported" vs. "as currently defined" toggle from continuity brief §55.5 is NOT part of this batch, it's query-time logic layered on top of this table by whatever screen consumes Service Alignment for LOB grouping (Forecast, most likely), and doesn't block CRUD on the table itself. Worker batch v1f (2026-09-05): fixed suggestGrowthPct on the Regions screen. It was raw YoY rounded with no bound, so a line of business with a small or volatile prior-year base could suggest something like +766% or -900%, real math but a useless, distracting number for a growth target field. Caught live in the browser on Corporate (small LOB dollar bases) and confirmed it also shows up on large regions like NY Metro (Inspections +213% YoY there). Now clamped to [-50, 100]. This only changes the suggested starting value, it never touches a target a user has actually set, and the user can still type any number they want. Worker batch v1g (2026-09-05): account-mapping review routes. Confirmed directly against live D1 data that bookings_fact.parent_company (~221 distinct values) and backlog_fact.parent_customer_name (~2,184 distinct values) are different grains of the same relationship, not just inconsistent spelling, so an automatic join was rejected (a fuzzy substring/first-two-words heuristic wrongly matched "Partners" to 3 unrelated accounts in real data). Same pattern as region_raw_mappings: a real admin-curated mapping table, account_mappings, with any fuzzy match shown as a suggestion only, never auto-applied. Scope confirmed with Misty: review covers only the 89 bookings accounts (as of the 2026-09-05 analysis) with zero normalized match in backlog, not the full backlog list. New routes: GET /api/account-mappings/review, GET /api/account-mappings/search-backlog, POST /api/account-mappings. The account_mappings table itself was already created live in D1 before this batch, verified via PRAGMA table_info against the exact schema proposed. Worker batch v1h (2026-09-06): Top Accounts by Service Line's Opportunity column, confirmed with Misty against the live screen. She wanted the missing services actually named, ranked by value, not just a count. getRegionsScreen's largestAccounts now carries two ranked lists per account, whitespaceRegion (missing service families sorted by that family's own $ total in THIS region this year) and whitespaceCompany (same ranking, but against every region's total, i.e. VDA-wide), top 3 each, via the new rankMissingFamilies helper. regionFamilyTotals is derived from the same accountRows query already run for the account breakdown, no extra round trip, companyFamilyTotals needed one new unscoped query. Purely informational, same as every other suggestion in this app, never auto-applied. Worker batch v1i (2026-09-06): Account Mapping review rescoped per continuity brief §70, correcting an earlier mis-synthesis that would have silently excluded below-threshold accounts from dollar totals. The corrected design is two separate concepts: 1. A $5,000 lifetime-bookings threshold (ACCOUNT_MAPPING_REVIEW_ THRESHOLD) now scopes the review QUEUE only, confirmed against the real 89-account distribution ($522,806 total, $67,144.60 down to $286): $5,000+ surfaces 32 of 89 accounts. This threshold has no effect on any dollar total anywhere else in the app, it only decides which accounts an admin is asked to manually review. 2. A new account_mapping_reviews table (one column beyond its key: status, currently only ever 'no_match') records an account that was deliberately reviewed and found to have no Backlog match, distinct from an account that simply hasn't been looked at yet. getAccountMappingReview now returns both the above-threshold accounts (with this status folded in) and the below-threshold accounts (name + value only, for the frontend's collapsible panel), plus real totals for the intro copy. saveAccountMappings now also clears any no_match record for an account the moment a real mapping is confirmed for it, keeping the two states mutually exclusive. New routes: POST /api/account-mappings/no-match, POST /api/account-mappings/no-match/clear. Requires the new account_mapping_reviews D1 table (see sales/DEPLOY-NOTES.md for the migration statement, run individually per standing rule 6). Worker batch v1j (2026-09-06): Coverage upload, per claude/PULSE-coverage-upload-scoping.md (real Consultants.xlsx/ Inspectors.xlsx rosters inspected directly before designing this). Backs Slide 4's "Under-covered Tier 1 Markets" list, replacing getRegionsScreen's coverageAvailable: false placeholder with a real Haversine gap calculation against two new tables: - city_geocodes (state, city, lat, lng): 268 rows seeded once offline (119 city_market_tiers cities + 178 distinct roster locations), no live geocoding API, no runtime network calls. - coverage_roster (id, upload_batch_id, source_file, role, department_raw, city, state, region_id, counts_toward_coverage): one row per active, anonymized field-role person. Names are never written here, confirmed decision, stripped at ingestion. Two independent upload cards (Consultants, Inspectors), each replacing only its own source_file's rows on reupload (scoped delete + insert in one batch, same shape as replaceBilledFactForYear but keyed by source_file instead of fiscal_year). region_id is resolved from each row's state via state_region_map (NOT region_raw_mappings, which is Salesforce-raw-text-keyed and doesn't apply here, and NOT Inspectors' own department field, which holds legacy acquired-company names like Dominion/AllSafe rather than real regions). Coverage location: Consultants use Location Description when it parses as "City, ST" (a real assigned office/market hub), falling back to home city/state otherwise; Inspectors always use home city/state (no second field exists). Only field-facing roles (Consultant, Area Manager, Field Examiner) count toward the gap calculation; Engineer/Sales/Senior VP/Regional VP rows are stored but excluded via counts_toward_coverage = 0. Coverage radius is a single constant, COVERAGE_RADIUS_MILES = 50, confirmed with Misty, not per-region. New routes: POST/GET /api/uploads/coverage-consultants, POST/GET /api/uploads/coverage-inspectors. Requires the new coverage_roster and city_geocodes D1 tables (see claude/PULSE-coverage-schema-v1.md for the migration statements, run individually per standing rule 6). New getGrowthStrategyReport(env, fiscalYear, regionId) function, reusing getRegionsScreen for base data (backlog by LOB, whitespace accounts via the existing ACCOUNT_SERVICE_FAMILIES/rankMissingFamilies machinery) and adding: Account Concentration (top 3 / top 10 accounts percent of region), Lowest-Selling Services (region vs. company-wide, preserving the Inspections-excluded-in-West/Northwest exception), Sales Volume Leaderboard, Proposal Win Rate (grouped by proposal_volume_fact's real service_name column directly, confirmed there is no service_family column to group by instead), Client Retention Rate and Account Tenure Mix (both real queries against bookings_fact, honestly scoped to the fiscal years with real upload history rather than a hardcoded year window), a computed, ranked Next $1M scoring table (Whitespace $, gap vs. company-wide benchmark, YoY trend, gated by retention), and a generated "Where to Focus" recommendation string synthesized from the report's own data. NOTE ON VERSIONING (2026-09-06): this code was first written directly into worker_v1j.mjs in place, a violation of this project's own copy-then-bump-before-editing rule, caught and corrected by Misty ("you need to bump the version on each code files, you're not following the rules"). worker_v1j.mjs is left as it was edited (not reverted) since an exact byte-level revert could not be verified from within that session; this file (v1k) and pulse_strategy_v1n.html are the real, correctly-versioned next files going forward, and v1j should not be treated as a separately deployed version. Worker batch v1l (2026-09-06): Growth Strategy Report bug-fix pass, found by Misty reviewing the live v1k/v1n deploy (real data exposed the bugs; nothing wrong with the deploy itself). Paired with pulse_strategy_v1p.html. Two real fixes, both inside getGrowthStrategyReport: 1. Next $1M whitespace $ duplication: the whitespace figure for each service family was computed by summing missing accounts' whole- account revenue (total across every service they DO buy), not anything specific to the missing family. Families sharing the same set of missing accounts got the identical, inflated whitespace $ and vs.-company-benchmark numbers (caught directly: three families showing the exact same "$382K (5 accts)"). Fixed to a real per-family estimate: a new companyFamilyAvgPerAccount lookup (companyFamilyTotals[family] / distinct company-wide accounts that buy it, confirmed with Misty as the right basis) times the number of region accounts missing that family. accountsMissingFamily's account list/ranking (used for topWhitespaceAccounts, "who to pitch") is unchanged and correct, only the $ aggregate was wrong. 2. Revenue Trend YoY: revenueTrend only ever computed one YoY% across all three lines of business combined (byLob's dollar figures were always correct; the single yoyPct was not broken out per LOB at all). Added a real byLobYoy per line of business, same calculation as the total-level yoyPct, computed per LOB. Worker batch v1m (2026-09-06): Revenue Budget annual grid reversed from independently-editable to DERIVED from Monthly Detail, per Misty's explicit instruction ("monthly should drive annual now" — reverses §55.2's original "keep them independent" call, which this file's own comments used to describe at length; see listRevenueBudgetsAnnualDerived's comment for the full history). Paired with pulse_strategy_v1q.html. - New listRevenueBudgetsAnnualDerived(env, fiscalYear) helper: sums revenue_budgets_monthly per region/LOB. One shared implementation used by (a) GET /api/baseline/revenue-budgets (now returns derived rows instead of reading the old revenue_budgets table) and (b) both Forecast call sites (Month Pace, Regional Drivers) that used to read revenue_budgets directly as their annual-total fallback — kept in one place so "annual total" can't drift between the screen you enter it on and the screens that just display it. - POST /api/baseline/revenue-budgets now returns a 400 rather than writing — the annual cells are read-only in the frontend now, this is a fail-safe for a stale cached page mid-transition, not an expected live path. - revenue_budgets (the old table) is left in place, unused, rather than dropped — a D1 table drop isn't reversible and nothing currently requires deleting it. - A region/LOB with zero monthly rows derives to $0, no fallback to whatever was previously typed into the old annual table, confirmed directly: "some are entered as 0 meaning they didn't have a budget," so absence and an explicit $0 both mean the same thing now. Worker batch v1n (2026-09-06): new POST /api/baseline/revenue-budgets- monthly-batch route (upsertRevenueBudgetMonthlyCellsBatch), backing pulse_strategy_v1r.html's paste-a-row feature — Misty copies a row straight out of her real Excel gating workbook (Jan-Dec, tab- separated) and pastes it into one Monthly Detail cell instead of typing each month by hand. Takes the same region/LOB/fiscalYear as the single-cell route plus a cells array ({month, amount} pairs, capped at 12 per request), validates every cell up front, then writes them all in one env.DB.batch() call — atomic, and one round trip instead of up to 12 sequential POSTs. Added to the isBaselineRoute admin-gate list alongside the existing revenue-budgets-monthly route, so it gets the same requireAdmin check (the §5 security review's fix for these routes previously only requiring a signed-in session). Worker batch v1o (2026-09-06): range-select + Delete on data-entry grids, per Misty's direct request ("select multiple fields and delete all entries"). Two new batch-clear routes, both admin-gated like their sibling routes: - POST /api/baseline/revenue-budgets-monthly-clear (clearRevenueBudgetMonthlyCellsBatch): clears a set of {lineOfBusiness, month} cells within one region/fiscalYear on Monthly Detail. Kept separate from the existing paste-batch route/function rather than reused, since a selected range can span multiple LOB rows (paste is always one row, capped at 12 months) — this one caps at 36 (every LOB x every month for one region). - POST /api/baseline/sales-budgets-clear (clearBudgetCellsBatch, generic over VALID_BUDGET_TABLES like upsertBudgetCell): clears a set of {regionId, lineOfBusiness} cells within one fiscalYear on the Sales Budgets annual grid, capped at 100 cells. Rejects revenue_budgets explicitly (that grid is derived/read-only, same as the existing single-cell POST route) even though the function is generic, so it can't be pointed at the wrong table by mistake. Both routes write amount:0 via the same INSERT...ON CONFLICT...DO UPDATE pattern as their single-cell counterparts, in one env.DB.batch() call per request — atomic, all cells in a selection land together. Worker batch v1p (2026-09-06): "Suggest from history" for Monthly Detail, per Misty's direct request: "recommend a monthly breakdown based on historical monthly budget and actual revenue attained that month to make the seasonal budget influenced by historical actuals." New read-only GET /api/baseline/revenue-budgets-monthly-suggest route (suggestRevenueBudgetMonthlyFromHistory, admin-gated like its sibling routes) — see that function's own comment block for the full formula. Summary: for one region (all 3 LOBs at once), blends prior fiscal year's monthly BUDGET % shape (revenue_budgets_monthly) with prior fiscal year's monthly ACTUALS % shape (billed_fact, joined to LOB via the existing mapRevenueTypeToLob helper) 50/50 — or 100% of whichever exists if only one does, confirmed directly with Misty — then applies that blended shape to prior fiscal year's real actual annual total for that region/LOB (there is no separate "new year target" to spread; confirmed directly that using last year's real total as the starting point is correct). Returns suggested amounts ONLY for months with no existing row (or an explicit $0) in the CURRENT fiscal year being edited — a month with real data entry is never included in the response at all, so the frontend can't touch it even by mistake. This is a read-only computation: nothing is written to the database by this route; the frontend fills suggested values into blank cells and they're saved the normal way (or edited first), same as typed or pasted entry. Worker batch v1q (2026-09-06): Line of Business resolution for Slide 3 ("Line of Business Detail," Growth Strategy Report). Scoping this surfaced a real gap: service_alignment (meant to map bookings_fact's 9 service families to the 3-way program_advisory/design_services/ inspections taxonomy used by Budgets) is confirmed empty in production (live SELECT * returned no rows) and was never actually read by any reporting query, only by its own CRUD screen. Fixed at the root: - New DEFAULT_LOB_MAP + defaultLineOfBusiness(): a real fallback mapping built family-by-family from Misty's direct assignments, not a generic taxonomy. Construction Services is name-level (confirmed via live query of its 14 real service_name values): "Design Services Site Visit" -> design_services, everything else in that family -> program_advisory. Equipment Evaluation carries both real spellings for Quality Control Evaluation ("Quality Control Evaluation" and "QCE") -> program_advisory for now; Misty flagged this should move to inspections starting FY2027, which is exactly what a service_alignment override (below) is for rather than a second hardcoded map. - New resolveLineOfBusiness(serviceFamily, serviceName, fiscalYear, alignmentRows): checks service_alignment for an exact-year override first (name-level, then family-level), falls back to defaultLineOfBusiness() only when no override exists. This makes service_alignment real and load-bearing for the first time, while keeping today's zero-row table a no-op (pure fallback to defaults) until Misty adds real overrides — such as the QCE-to-inspections change for FY2027. - New GET /api/baseline/service-alignment-defaults route (admin-gated, added to isBaselineRoute), returns DEFAULT_LOB_MAP as plain rows. Per Misty's explicit requirement ("we should have this table visible in the UI so we can make adjustments if I made bad assumptions"), this powers a new read-only Defaults table on the Service Alignment screen rather than leaving the mapping buried in code. - getGrowthStrategyReport gains lineOfBusinessDetail: zeroAttachAccounts (largest accounts billing Inspections with zero Program Advisory, top 5 by Inspections $), inspectionAttach (% of Inspections accounts that also buy Program Advisory), inspectionRetention (% of prior- year Inspections accounts still buying Inspections this year, same account-identity logic as the existing Client Retention Rate), and softestRenewalSegments (backlog_fact.vertical_end_market segments with the lowest prior-year-to-this-year Inspections account retention, minimum 2 prior-year accounts to be included, top 5 lowest-retention first). All four computed from real bookings_fact/ backlog_fact rows via resolveLineOfBusiness, not the existing mapServiceToAccountFamily (that's a 9-bucket taxonomy for other cards on this report, not real LOB grain). Note backlog_fact keys accounts by parent_customer_name while bookings_fact uses parent_company for the same real-world account — confirmed these are not coincidentally the same column name before joining across them for the retention-by-segment metric. worker_v1r (2026-09-06): Revenue Bridge and Executive Summary, both new screens this segment, following review of a ChatGPT "GrowthOS" mockup (Growth Bridge and Assumption Register were the only two ideas judged worth adopting; the rest, an Opportunity Score formula, a six-stage taxonomy, a generic nav, rejected as consultant theater inconsistent with this app's real-data discipline). - New getRevenueBridge(env, fiscalYear, regionId): a real 7-step build-up from YTD Actuals to Expected Revenue (§19 methodology). regionId nullable (null = company-wide "All Regions"). Steps: YTD Actuals (billed_fact), Inspections Run Rate (computeBlendedForecast, reused verbatim), Program Advisory Backlog, Design Services Backlog (both backlog_fact, end_date in next fiscal year), Weighted Pipeline (proposal_pipeline_fact, one combined step across LOBs per Misty's choice — that table has no LOB column to split by), Proposal Volume x Win Rate (proposal_volume_fact, real 63,398-row upload, trailing 12 months of proposal_issued_date, all-stage volume base times a Closed-Won/(Won+Lost) win rate, PA+DS only to avoid double-counting Inspections' own run-rate step — all three Misty's explicit, confirmed choices), Expected Revenue (sum). Also returns a reconciliation block (Expected Revenue vs. stated Revenue Budget) as a bonus check beyond the original spec. proposal_issued_date's real M/D/YYYY format (not lexically sortable) was verified live before writing PROPOSAL_ISSUED_YEAR_SQL/MONTH_SQL/YM_SQL, not assumed from the other date columns' formats. - New GET /api/regions/revenue-bridge?fiscalYear=®ionId= route (regionId omitted or "all" = company-wide). - New getExecutiveSummary(env, fiscalYear): composes three pieces via Promise.all — getForecastMonthPace, getForecastRegionalDrivers, and getRevenueBridge(fiscalYear, null) — reused rather than recomputed, after confirming with Misty that "Gavin's summary tables" already exist as these two real Forecast functions. Adds a new company-wide Service Family chart (bookings_fact, the existing 9-family ACCOUNT_SERVICE_FAMILIES/mapServiceToAccountFamily taxonomy already used on Regions' Biggest Accounts card, applied here company-wide). - Company-wide Revenue pace (revenueAvailable/revenuePace), added after Misty's mockup review flagged the pace tiles as too Sales- heavy: sums the per-region Revenue figures getForecastRegionalDrivers already computes from billed_fact (regions[].revenue), no new query. null when zero regions have billed data yet, matching the same "explicit pending state, never a fake number" rule used elsewhere. - buildExecutiveNarrative(...): auto-generated "This Month at a Glance" summary, confirmed via AskUserQuestion as auto-generated from real figures already on the page rather than an editable free- text field. Every line is templated off fields already computed above (monthPace, revenuePace, regionalDrivers, revenueBridge. reconciliation) — Sales pace, Revenue pace, the region furthest below its own Sales pace and its named LOB/Service-Family shortfall driver, the region furthest below its own Revenue pace and its driver (a separate sort from the Sales one — not necessarily the same region), and the Revenue Bridge's reconciliation vs. budget. A line is omitted, never guessed at, when its figure isn't available yet. - New GET /api/executive-summary?fiscalYear= route. worker_v1s (2026-09-06): Revenue Bridge Step 5 (Weighted Pipeline) bug fix. Misty, live-testing Executive Summary: "the expected revenue is ridiculously too high on the bridge... are you looking at expected award date." Checked the real code: Step 5 had no filter on estimated_award_date at all, and no exclusion of Closed Won/Closed Lost rows -- it summed every row in proposal_pipeline_fact with a fee and probability, regardless of when that opportunity is expected to close or whether it's even still open. Verified live before fixing (Misty ran the query): real estimated_award_date values include a stale '2/27/2020' row and a next-fiscal-year '1/11/2027' row, so the bug was real and material, not theoretical. New ESTIMATED_AWARD_YEAR_SQL (same M/D/YYYY SUBSTR extraction as the other date columns, verified against this exact column before use, not assumed from the others). Step 5 now: (1) only rows whose estimated_award_date falls within the fiscal year being bridged, and (2) excludes stage IN ('Closed Won', 'Closed Lost') -- confirmed with Misty via AskUserQuestion that Closed Won is already counted in YTD Actuals (double-counting risk) and Closed Lost isn't pipeline at all. worker_v1t (2026-09-06): Revenue Bridge Step 6 (Proposal Volume x Win Rate) bug fix, same live-testing pass -- Misty: "still way too high. the budget expectation is $137M - you're projecting almost 3x that." A second, distinct bug from Step 5's: volumeBase summed EVERY proposal line issued in the trailing 12 months regardless of stage, then multiplied that whole population by a win rate and added the result as new Expected Revenue -- but "every proposal issued in the last 12 months" is overwhelmingly proposals that have already resolved (already won, and so already counted in Step 1 YTD Actuals or Steps 3/4 Backlog, or already lost and never real revenue at all), so this was double/triple-counting most of a year of proposal activity on top of the other steps. Confirmed the fix with Misty via AskUserQuestion before writing it: the win rate itself still needs the full resolved trailing-12-months population (Closed Won / (Closed Won + Closed Lost)) to be meaningful, but the volume base multiplied by that rate is now ONLY the still-open portion (every stage except Closed Won/Closed Lost) -- the part that actually represents real, not-yet-counted future business. volumeByLob's `all` field renamed to `open` to make this explicit in the code itself, not just the comment. worker_v1t (2026-09-06, same pass): Revenue Bridge Step 5 stale- pipeline refinement. Even after the Step 5 date/stage fix above, Misty spotted a remaining data-quality issue while reviewing the numbers: "if it has estimated award dates that have passed, it's a good chance the team did list as lost" -- i.e. an open-stage row whose estimated_award_date is already in the past is very likely a deal the sales team simply never updated to Closed Won/Closed Lost, not real live pipeline. Verified live before changing anything (Misty ran the query): of the 4,566 open, FY2026-award-dated rows Step 5 was summing, 2,985 of them ($36.08M of $75.26M weighted) had an estimated_award_date already in the past -- essentially half the dollar value of Step 5 was resting on likely-stale rows. Rather than excluding every past-due row outright, Misty specified an exact rule: "I think we only include 60 days past due otherwise exclude. This means it will change monthly." A 60-day-past-due row is still plausibly a live deal running slightly behind schedule; a row more than 60 days past its estimated award date with no stage update is not credible as open pipeline. New PIPELINE_PAST_DUE_GRACE_DAYS = 60 constant. This is a *rolling* window measured from `asOf` (this app's real, data-driven "today" derived from MAX(awarded_date) in bookings_fact, already used elsewhere for forecast math) -- not a fixed calendar cutoff -- so a row ages out of the bridge on its own as time passes unless the sales team refreshes it, exactly as Misty intended ("it will change monthly"). Implementation: Step 5 now fetches raw total_vda_fees/probability_pct/estimated_award_date rows (same WHERE clause as the v1s fix) and does the day-level past-due math in JS with real Date objects, rather than attempting fragile day-arithmetic SQL against the non-standard M/D/YYYY text column. Excluded count and dollar value are surfaced (not silently dropped) via detail.pipelineStaleExcluded = { count, value, graceDays }, so the exclusion is auditable in the app itself. worker_v1u (2026-09-06): Revenue Bridge Step 2 (Inspections Run Rate) double-count fix. Misty re-tested live after v1t deployed and reported it was "still way too high," adding "Inspection run rate seems high too as a side note" and separately "I think the inspections budget for the whole year was $60M" -- a live screenshot showed the Inspections Run Rate bar alone at +$59.63M, i.e. roughly the entire year's Inspections budget packed into what should be an incremental step. Checked the real code: Step 2 was `inspectionsRunRate = inspectionsForecast.totalYearForecast`, which is priorActualsSum + currentMonthBlended + remainingMonthsForecast -- the WHOLE fiscal year's Inspections total, actuals included. But Step 1 (YTD Actuals, immediately above it in the same function) sums every LOB's real billed_fact dollars through the current month, Inspections included, via the exact same `monthlyByLob.inspections` data. So Step 2 was re-adding all of Inspections' already-counted YTD actuals on top of Step 1, then adding the forecasted remainder as new money as well -- a plain double-count once traced through, not a forecasting/modeling bug. Confirmed the fix shape via AskUserQuestion before writing it (two options: make Step 2 remaining-months-only, or keep Step 2 as the full-year total and strip Inspections out of Step 1 instead); Misty chose the former, the standard incremental run-rate meaning. Step 2 is now `totalYearForecast - priorActualsSum - currentMonthActuals`, which collapses to exactly the not-yet-actual portion of the year: the forecasted remainder of the current month plus the average-based projection for the fully future months -- nothing that Step 1 already counted. detail.inspectionsRunRate was also reshaped from `{ totalYearForecast, priorAvgPerMonth }` to `{ remainingYearForecast, fullYearForecast, priorAvgPerMonth }` so the whole-year figure Misty was sanity-checking against her $60M number stays visible for reference, without it looking like Step 2's actual contribution to Expected Revenue. worker_v2a (2026-09-06): Revenue Bridge Steps 5/6 double-count fix (past the v1-letter series, per this project's versioning rule -- next letter after v1z is v2a, not v1za, to avoid an ambiguous-looking filename). Misty re-tested live yet again after v1u deployed and asked directly: "is there anything else getting double counted? bridge still seems off" -- a live screenshot showed Expected Revenue at $258.76M against a $122.9M budget, still ~2.1x over. Investigated the two remaining un-audited dollar-contributing steps (5 and 6) together rather than one at a time, since three straight single-step fixes hadn't resolved it. Root cause, confirmed against the real schema doc before writing any code: proposal_pipeline_fact (Step 5, opportunity grain, per sales/schema.sql's own comment) and proposal_volume_fact (Step 6, line-item grain) are two DIFFERENT EXPORTS OF THE SAME UNDERLYING SALESFORCE OPPORTUNITIES, not two independent business populations -- the schema doc calls proposal_volume_fact "distinct from proposal_pipeline_fact's open- pipeline-weighted-by-probability input," but distinct data source is not the same as distinct dollars. Verified live before touching code (Misty ran the query): `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM proposal_pipeline_fact pp WHERE pp.stage NOT IN ('Closed Won', 'Closed Lost') AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM proposal_volume_fact pv WHERE pv.opportunity_name = pp.opportunity_name)` returned 4,462 -- a large, material overlap, not a coincidence. So an open deal could be weighted into Expected Revenue via Step 5 (its real per- opportunity probability_pct x fee) AND via Step 6 (a win-rate- weighted share of the same dollars), double-counting the same opportunity twice under two different formulas. Confirmed the fix via AskUserQuestion before writing it: rather than attempt an opportunity_name anti-join between two independently- uploaded weekly exports (fragile -- no guarantee an opportunity is named identically in both files every week, and no shared id column exists between them per the schema), Step 6's DOLLAR CONTRIBUTION is dropped from Expected Revenue entirely; Step 5's real per-opportunity probability remains the sole open-pipeline-value source for PA+DS. Step 6 still computes and returns the real Win Rate % (Closed Won / (Closed Won + Closed Lost)) and volume base via `paVolume`/`dsVolume` in `detail` -- useful context for the UI, e.g. the Growth Strategy Report's own Proposal Win Rate section (§85's methodology, unrelated to this bridge) -- it just no longer adds a dollar figure into Expected Revenue. `expectedRevenue` no longer sums `paVolume.contribution + dsVolume.contribution` (that field was removed from `volumeTimesWinRate`'s return value entirely, so it can't be accidentally re-added later), and the `proposal_volume_ win_rate` entry was removed from the `steps` array the frontend waterfall renders. ============================================================ */ // ---- Constants ----------------------------------------------------- const SESSION_TTL_SECONDS = 60 * 60 * 12; // 12h, same as Pricing const RESET_TOKEN_TTL_SECONDS = 60 * 60 * 24; // 24h, same as Pricing const PBKDF2_ITERATIONS = 100_000; const VALID_ROLES = ['admin']; // Strategy: admin-only for now (brief §10) const VALID_LOBS = ['program_advisory', 'design_services', 'inspections']; const PUBLIC_PATHS = new Set(['/login', '/health']); // ---- PULSE Hub SSO (2026-09-13) -------------------------------------- // // Same real pattern as Modernization's live handleSsoLogin, built after // tracing Modernization's actual worker/client code and confirming with // Misty how Strategy's own, different auth model should adapt it, rather // than assuming Strategy mirrors Modernization directly. Two confirmed // differences from Modernization, both deliberate: // // 1. Gate is Hub Admins only, full stop. Strategy's own VALID_ROLES is // ['admin'] today (see file header) — there is no non-admin role to // grant yet, so this does NOT check hubUser.access.strategy the way // Modernization checks access.modernization. Misty confirmed // directly (2026-09-13): "let's go with option 1 but eventually // Strategy will have an 'executive' role" — when that role exists, // a real HUB_ROLE_TO_STRATEGY_ROLE-style mapping keyed off // hubUser.access.strategy belongs here, matching Modernization's own // HUB_ROLE_TO_MOD_ROLE. Until then, gating on hubRole === 'Admin' // alone is the correct behavior, not a placeholder. // 2. No Access PINs lookup, no role picker. Modernization looks up a // Airtable "Access PINs" record and falls back to the Hub's own // name/email when nothing matches. Strategy's local identity is a // real D1 `users` row (id/name/email/role/active), provisioned ahead // of time by an admin via createUser — there is no equivalent // "log in anyway with no linked record" concept, and Misty confirmed // directly (2026-09-13, "Deny access (Recommended)") that SSO should // NOT auto-provision or fall back: a Hub Admin with no matching, // active users row is denied with a clear message, exactly like // handleLogin already denies an unknown/inactive name today. Matched // by email first (more specific), then by name, both case- // insensitive (COLLATE NOCASE, matching every other users lookup in // this file) — never invented as a new user. // // Fetches the Hub's own /session endpoint through the HUB_API Service // Binding, not a plain fetch() to its public URL — a plain fetch() from // one Cloudflare Worker to another Cloudflare-proxied domain is rejected // with Cloudflare error 1042 ("request originated from a Cloudflare // IP"), discovered and fixed this same way on Modernization's real, // live worker (see Modernization's own handleSsoLogin comment for the // full debugging history). Built with the Service Binding from the // start here rather than rediscovering that bug a second time — falls // back to a plain fetch() only if the binding isn't configured yet, so // a missing binding fails with a clear, actionable message instead of a // confusing generic "session invalid" error. const HUB_WORKER_URL = 'https://pulse-hub-api.misty-munoz.workers.dev'; // v3i (2026-09-13): No functional change to this file. Paired with // client pulse_strategy_v3j.html, which fixes a client-side bug where // tryHubSso() checked a `result.ok` field this worker's success // responses never send (every login path here, including this one, // returns { token, user }, never { ok }) — traced live via DevTools // (200 response, correct body, client still fell through to the login // screen). Confirmed via handleSsoLogin below and the shared json() // helper further down that the worker's contract was already correct; // this version bump exists only to keep client/worker release numbers // paired for this fix. async function handleSsoLogin(request, env) { const body = await request.json().catch(() => ({})); const hubToken = (body.hubToken || '').trim(); if (!hubToken) return jsonError('Missing Hub session.', 400); // Same rate-limit bucket real login failures already use (name+IP), // keyed under a fixed pseudo-name so a flood of invalid SSO attempts // from one IP can't be used to bypass the per-name lockout entirely. const withinLimit = await checkLoginRateLimit(env, '__sso__', request); if (!withinLimit) return jsonError('Too many attempts. Try again in a few minutes.', 429); let hubData; try { const hubRes = env.HUB_API ? await env.HUB_API.fetch(`${HUB_WORKER_URL}/session`, { headers: { Authorization: 'Bearer ' + hubToken } }) : await fetch(`${HUB_WORKER_URL}/session`, { headers: { Authorization: 'Bearer ' + hubToken } }); const hubBodyText = await hubRes.text(); if (!hubRes.ok) { await recordLoginFailure(env, '__sso__', request); const notConfigured = !env.HUB_API; return jsonError( notConfigured ? 'PULSE Hub sign-in is not fully configured yet (missing HUB_API service binding). Contact IT.' : 'Your Hub session is no longer valid. Please sign in again.', notConfigured ? 500 : 401 ); } try { hubData = JSON.parse(hubBodyText); } catch (e) { return jsonError('Could not verify your Hub session. Check your connection and try again.', 502); } } catch (e) { return jsonError('Could not verify your Hub session. Check your connection and try again.', 502); } if (!hubData || !hubData.ok || !hubData.user) { await recordLoginFailure(env, '__sso__', request); return jsonError('Your Hub session is no longer valid. Please sign in again.', 401); } const hubUser = hubData.user; if (hubUser.hubRole !== 'Admin') { // No non-admin Strategy role exists yet (VALID_ROLES, see file // header) — until Strategy has an "executive" role backed by // hubUser.access.strategy, only Hub Admins may SSO in at all. return jsonError('You do not have access to PULSE Strategy.', 403); } const emailLower = (hubUser.email || '').trim().toLowerCase(); const nameLower = (hubUser.name || '').trim().toLowerCase(); let user = null; if (emailLower) { user = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT * FROM users WHERE email = ? COLLATE NOCASE').bind(emailLower).first(); } if (!user && nameLower) { user = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT * FROM users WHERE name = ? COLLATE NOCASE').bind(nameLower).first(); } if (!user || !user.active) { // Deny, never auto-provision — confirmed directly with Misty // (2026-09-13): every Strategy user is provisioned ahead of time by // an admin via createUser, and SSO should not bypass that. await recordLoginFailure(env, '__sso__', request); return jsonError("You don't have a PULSE Strategy account yet. Contact your admin.", 403); } // Real credentials verified via the Hub — clear this bucket's failure // count, same as a successful manual login does. await clearLoginFailures(env, '__sso__', request); const token = randomToken(32); const expiresAt = new Date(Date.now() + SESSION_TTL_SECONDS * 1000).toISOString(); await env.DB.prepare( 'INSERT INTO sessions (token, user_id, expires_at) VALUES (?, ?, ?)' ).bind(token, user.id, expiresAt).run(); await env.SESSIONS_KV.put(token, JSON.stringify({ userId: user.id, name: user.name, role: user.role }), { expirationTtl: SESSION_TTL_SECONDS, }); await env.DB.prepare('UPDATE users SET last_login_at = ? WHERE id = ?') .bind(new Date().toISOString(), user.id).run(); return json({ token, user: { id: user.id, name: user.name, role: user.role } }); } // Login rate limiting (security review, 2026-09-02): the one-time PIN // issued on account creation/reset is only 6 numeric digits (900,000 // possibilities, see generatePin() below) — with no attempt cap, that // keyspace is crackable well inside an hour by an unthrottled attacker // who knows a valid account name. Tracked in SESSIONS_KV (no new binding // needed) keyed by name+IP, so a lockout on one name doesn't block every // other user, and a lockout from one IP doesn't block a legitimate user // behind a shared/NAT'd IP trying a different (correct) name. const LOGIN_MAX_ATTEMPTS = 8; const LOGIN_LOCKOUT_SECONDS = 15 * 60; // 15 minutes // ---- Crypto helpers (same approach as Pricing) ---------------------- function toHex(buf) { return [...new Uint8Array(buf)].map(b => b.toString(16).padStart(2, '0')).join(''); } function fromHex(hex) { const bytes = new Uint8Array(hex.length / 2); for (let i = 0; i < bytes.length; i++) bytes[i] = parseInt(hex.substr(i * 2, 2), 16); return bytes; } function randomToken(bytes = 32) { const arr = new Uint8Array(bytes); crypto.getRandomValues(arr); return toHex(arr); } async function hashPin(pin, saltHex) { const salt = fromHex(saltHex); const keyMaterial = await crypto.subtle.importKey( 'raw', new TextEncoder().encode(pin), { name: 'PBKDF2' }, false, ['deriveBits'] ); const bits = await crypto.subtle.deriveBits( { name: 'PBKDF2', salt, iterations: PBKDF2_ITERATIONS, hash: 'SHA-256' }, keyMaterial, 256 ); return toHex(bits); } async function verifyPin(pin, storedHashHex, storedSaltHex) { const computed = await hashPin(pin, storedSaltHex); if (computed.length !== storedHashHex.length) return false; let diff = 0; for (let i = 0; i < computed.length; i++) diff |= computed.charCodeAt(i) ^ storedHashHex.charCodeAt(i); return diff === 0; } function generatePin() { // 6-digit numeric one-time PIN, same as Pricing. const arr = new Uint32Array(1); crypto.getRandomValues(arr); return String(100000 + (arr[0] % 900000)); } // ---- Email (Resend, same visual template as Pricing's real, live PIN // email — dark card, logo badge, portal link, sign-in button — with // Strategy's own purple accent (#4a3aa7) in place of Pricing's teal. // Confirmed against a real sent Pricing email in the Resend dashboard, // 2026-09-03, not re-derived from guesswork. One deliberate structural // difference: Pricing's template has a "Your Role" row because Pricing // has an admin/sales split; Strategy currently has only the admin role // (VALID_ROLES, see file header), so that row would always read "Admin" // and add nothing — dropped rather than carried over verbatim. ------- const PORTAL_URL = 'elevatorpulse.com/strategy'; const PIN_EMAIL_COPY = { welcome: { subject: 'Your PULSE Strategy PIN', eyebrow: 'Welcome to PULSE Strategy', heading: 'Your account is ready', intro: (name) => `Hi ${name}, an administrator created a PULSE Strategy account for you. Use the one-time PIN below to sign in, then choose your own permanent PIN.`, pinRowLabel: 'One-time PIN', buttonLabel: 'Sign in to PULSE Strategy', footerUrgent: 'This PIN works once. If you did not expect this account, contact your PULSE Strategy administrator.', }, reset: { subject: 'Your PULSE Strategy PIN was reset', eyebrow: 'PULSE Strategy', heading: 'Your PIN was reset', intro: (name) => `Hi ${name}, an administrator reset your PIN. Use the one-time PIN below to sign in, then choose a new permanent PIN. You have been signed out everywhere.`, pinRowLabel: 'New PIN', buttonLabel: 'Sign in to PULSE Strategy', footerUrgent: 'If you did not request this, contact your PULSE Strategy administrator right away.', }, resend: { subject: 'Your PULSE Strategy PIN', eyebrow: 'PULSE Strategy', heading: "Here's your new PIN", intro: (name) => `Hi ${name}, as requested, here's a fresh one-time PIN so you can get back into PULSE Strategy. Use the details below to sign back in.`, pinRowLabel: 'New PIN', buttonLabel: 'Sign in to PULSE Strategy', footerUrgent: 'If you did not request this, contact your PULSE Strategy administrator right away.', }, }; async function sendPinEmail(env, user, pin, { kind }) { const copy = PIN_EMAIL_COPY[kind] || PIN_EMAIL_COPY.welcome; const accent = '#4a3aa7'; const html = `
S
PULSE Strategy
${copy.eyebrow}

${copy.heading}

${copy.intro(user.name)}

${copy.pinRowLabel}
${pin}
Portal
${PORTAL_URL}

This is a one-time PIN. When you sign in with it, you'll be asked to choose your own permanent PIN right away. This one stops working once you do.

${copy.buttonLabel}

This notification was sent from ${env.RESEND_FROM_EMAIL}. ${copy.footerUrgent}

`; try { const res = await fetch('https://api.resend.com/emails', { method: 'POST', headers: { 'Authorization': `Bearer ${env.RESEND_API_KEY}`, 'Content-Type': 'application/json', }, body: JSON.stringify({ from: env.RESEND_FROM_EMAIL, to: user.email, subject: copy.subject, html, }), }); if (!res.ok) { const errText = await res.text().catch(() => ''); return { ok: false, error: `Resend ${res.status}: ${errText}` }; } return { ok: true }; } catch (e) { return { ok: false, error: String(e) }; } } async function issueAndSendPin(env, user, { kind }) { const pin = generatePin(); const saltHex = randomToken(16); const hashHex = await hashPin(pin, saltHex); await env.DB.prepare( 'UPDATE users SET pin_hash = ?, pin_salt = ?, must_reset_pin = 1 WHERE id = ?' ).bind(hashHex, saltHex, user.id).run(); await revokeAllSessionsForUser(env, user.id); const emailResult = await sendPinEmail(env, user, pin, { kind }); await env.DB.prepare( 'UPDATE users SET last_pin_email_status = ?, last_pin_email_at = ? WHERE id = ?' ).bind(emailResult.ok ? 'sent' : 'failed', new Date().toISOString(), user.id).run(); return { ok: true, emailed: emailResult.ok, emailError: emailResult.ok ? null : emailResult.error, fallbackPin: pin, // surfaced to admin UI only when emailed === false; never logged elsewhere }; } // ---- Login rate limiting (security review, 2026-09-02) -------------- // Keyed by name+IP so a lockout on one account name doesn't block every // other user sharing that IP, and a noisy IP trying many different // (correct) names doesn't lock out a legitimate user on a shared/NAT'd // connection who is only ever trying their own name. function loginAttemptKey(name, request) { const ip = request.headers.get('CF-Connecting-IP') || 'unknown'; return `login_attempts:${name.toLowerCase()}:${ip}`; } async function checkLoginRateLimit(env, name, request) { const key = loginAttemptKey(name, request); const raw = await env.SESSIONS_KV.get(key); const count = raw ? parseInt(raw, 10) || 0 : 0; return count < LOGIN_MAX_ATTEMPTS; } async function recordLoginFailure(env, name, request) { const key = loginAttemptKey(name, request); const raw = await env.SESSIONS_KV.get(key); const count = (raw ? parseInt(raw, 10) || 0 : 0) + 1; await env.SESSIONS_KV.put(key, String(count), { expirationTtl: LOGIN_LOCKOUT_SECONDS }); } async function clearLoginFailures(env, name, request) { const key = loginAttemptKey(name, request); await env.SESSIONS_KV.delete(key); } // ---- Auth handlers (login / set-pin / logout / session lookup) ------ async function handleLogin(request, env) { const body = await request.json().catch(() => ({})); const name = (body.name || '').trim(); const pin = (body.pin || '').trim(); if (!name || !pin) return jsonError('Enter your name and PIN to continue.', 400); const withinLimit = await checkLoginRateLimit(env, name, request); if (!withinLimit) { return jsonError('Too many attempts. Try again in a few minutes.', 429); } const user = await env.DB.prepare( 'SELECT * FROM users WHERE name = ? COLLATE NOCASE' ).bind(name).first(); if (!user || !user.active) { await recordLoginFailure(env, name, request); return jsonError('Sign in failed. Try again.', 401); } const valid = await verifyPin(pin, user.pin_hash, user.pin_salt); if (!valid) { await recordLoginFailure(env, name, request); return jsonError('Sign in failed. Try again.', 401); } // Real credentials verified — clear this name+IP's failure count so a // legitimate user who mistyped a few times isn't left partway toward // lockout on their next genuine attempt. await clearLoginFailures(env, name, request); if (user.must_reset_pin) { const resetToken = randomToken(32); const expiresAt = new Date(Date.now() + RESET_TOKEN_TTL_SECONDS * 1000).toISOString(); await env.DB.prepare( 'INSERT INTO reset_tokens (token, user_id, expires_at) VALUES (?, ?, ?)' ).bind(resetToken, user.id, expiresAt).run(); return json({ mustResetPin: true, resetToken, user: sanitizeUser(user) }); } const token = randomToken(32); const expiresAt = new Date(Date.now() + SESSION_TTL_SECONDS * 1000).toISOString(); await env.DB.prepare( 'INSERT INTO sessions (token, user_id, expires_at) VALUES (?, ?, ?)' ).bind(token, user.id, expiresAt).run(); await env.SESSIONS_KV.put(token, JSON.stringify({ userId: user.id, name: user.name, role: user.role }), { expirationTtl: SESSION_TTL_SECONDS, }); await env.DB.prepare('UPDATE users SET last_login_at = ? WHERE id = ?') .bind(new Date().toISOString(), user.id).run(); return json({ token, user: { id: user.id, name: user.name, role: user.role } }); } async function handleSetPin(request, env) { const body = await request.json().catch(() => ({})); const resetToken = (body.resetToken || '').trim(); const newPin = (body.newPin || '').trim(); if (!resetToken || !newPin) return jsonError('Missing reset token or PIN.', 400); if (newPin.length < 6 || !/[a-zA-Z]/.test(newPin) || !/[0-9]/.test(newPin)) { return jsonError('PIN must be at least 6 characters and contain at least one letter and one number.', 400); } const row = await env.DB.prepare( 'SELECT * FROM reset_tokens WHERE token = ?' ).bind(resetToken).first(); if (!row || row.used_at || new Date(row.expires_at) < new Date()) { return jsonError('This reset link is no longer valid. Contact your admin.', 401); } const user = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT * FROM users WHERE id = ?').bind(row.user_id).first(); if (!user || !user.active) return jsonError('This account is not set up for PULSE Strategy. Contact your admin.', 401); const saltHex = randomToken(16); const hashHex = await hashPin(newPin, saltHex); await env.DB.prepare( 'UPDATE users SET pin_hash = ?, pin_salt = ?, must_reset_pin = 0 WHERE id = ?' ).bind(hashHex, saltHex, user.id).run(); await env.DB.prepare('UPDATE reset_tokens SET used_at = ? WHERE token = ?') .bind(new Date().toISOString(), resetToken).run(); const token = randomToken(32); const expiresAt = new Date(Date.now() + SESSION_TTL_SECONDS * 1000).toISOString(); await env.DB.prepare( 'INSERT INTO sessions (token, user_id, expires_at) VALUES (?, ?, ?)' ).bind(token, user.id, expiresAt).run(); await env.SESSIONS_KV.put(token, JSON.stringify({ userId: user.id, name: user.name, role: user.role }), { expirationTtl: SESSION_TTL_SECONDS, }); await env.DB.prepare('UPDATE users SET last_login_at = ? WHERE id = ?') .bind(new Date().toISOString(), user.id).run(); return json({ token, user: { id: user.id, name: user.name, role: user.role } }); } async function handleLogout(request, env) { const token = bearerToken(request); if (token) { await env.SESSIONS_KV.delete(token); await env.DB.prepare('UPDATE sessions SET revoked_at = ? WHERE token = ?') .bind(new Date().toISOString(), token).run(); } return json({ ok: true }); } function bearerToken(request) { const auth = request.headers.get('Authorization') || ''; const m = auth.match(/^Bearer (.+)$/); return m ? m[1] : null; } async function getSession(request, env) { const token = bearerToken(request); if (!token) return null; const cached = await env.SESSIONS_KV.get(token); if (cached) { try { return { token, ...JSON.parse(cached) }; } catch (e) { /* fall through to D1 */ } } const row = await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT sessions.token, sessions.user_id, sessions.expires_at, sessions.revoked_at, users.name, users.role, users.active FROM sessions JOIN users ON users.id = sessions.user_id WHERE sessions.token = ?` ).bind(token).first(); if (!row || row.revoked_at || !row.active) return null; if (new Date(row.expires_at) < new Date()) return null; return { token, userId: row.user_id, name: row.name, role: row.role }; } async function requireSession(request, env) { const session = await getSession(request, env); if (!session) return { session: null, response: jsonError('Not signed in.', 401) }; return { session, response: null }; } async function requireAdmin(request, env) { const guard = await requireSession(request, env); if (guard.response) return guard; if (guard.session.role !== 'admin') { return { session: guard.session, response: jsonError('Admins only.', 403) }; } return guard; } function json(data, status = 200) { return new Response(JSON.stringify(data), { status, headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' } }); } function jsonError(message, status = 400) { return json({ error: message }, status); } // ---- User management (admin-only) ----------------------------------- function sanitizeUser(u) { return { id: u.id, name: u.name, email: u.email, role: u.role, active: !!u.active, created_at: u.created_at, last_login_at: u.last_login_at, }; } async function revokeAllSessionsForUser(env, userId) { const rows = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT token FROM sessions WHERE user_id = ? AND revoked_at IS NULL') .bind(userId).all(); for (const row of (rows.results || [])) { await env.SESSIONS_KV.delete(row.token); } await env.DB.prepare('UPDATE sessions SET revoked_at = ? WHERE user_id = ? AND revoked_at IS NULL') .bind(new Date().toISOString(), userId).run(); } async function countActiveAdmins(env, excludeUserId = null) { const row = await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT COUNT(*) as n FROM users WHERE role = 'admin' AND active = 1 AND id != ?` ).bind(excludeUserId || '').first(); return row ? row.n : 0; } async function listUsers(env) { const rows = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT * FROM users ORDER BY created_at DESC').all(); return (rows.results || []).map(sanitizeUser); } async function createUser(env, { name, email, role }, actorUserId) { name = (name || '').trim(); email = (email || '').trim(); if (!name) return { error: 'Name is required.', status: 400 }; if (!email) return { error: 'Email is required — the initial PIN is emailed, not typed in here.', status: 400 }; if (!isValidEmail(email)) return { error: 'That email address doesn\'t look valid.', status: 400 }; if (!VALID_ROLES.includes(role)) return { error: 'Invalid role.', status: 400 }; const existing = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT id FROM users WHERE name = ? COLLATE NOCASE').bind(name).first(); if (existing) return { error: 'A user with that name already exists.', status: 409 }; const id = randomToken(16); // Placeholder hash, immediately overwritten by issueAndSendPin below. const placeholderSalt = randomToken(16); const placeholderHash = await hashPin(randomToken(8), placeholderSalt); await env.DB.prepare( `INSERT INTO users (id, name, email, pin_hash, pin_salt, role, active, created_by) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, 1, ?)` ).bind(id, name, email, placeholderHash, placeholderSalt, role, actorUserId).run(); const user = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT * FROM users WHERE id = ?').bind(id).first(); const pinResult = await issueAndSendPin(env, user, { kind: 'welcome' }); return { ok: true, user: sanitizeUser(user), emailed: pinResult.emailed, emailError: pinResult.emailError, fallbackPin: pinResult.emailed ? undefined : pinResult.fallbackPin, }; } async function updateUser(env, id, { name, email, role, active }, actorUserId) { const user = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT * FROM users WHERE id = ?').bind(id).first(); if (!user) return { error: 'User not found.', status: 404 }; if (id === actorUserId && (active === false)) { return { error: 'You cannot deactivate your own account.', status: 400 }; } if (role !== undefined && !VALID_ROLES.includes(role)) { return { error: 'Invalid role.', status: 400 }; } const losingAdmin = (role !== undefined && role !== 'admin' && user.role === 'admin') || (active === false && user.role === 'admin' && user.active); if (losingAdmin) { const remaining = await countActiveAdmins(env, id); if (remaining < 1) return { error: 'Cannot remove the last active admin.', status: 400 }; } if (name !== undefined) { const trimmed = name.trim(); if (!trimmed) return { error: 'Name is required.', status: 400 }; const existing = await env.DB.prepare( 'SELECT id FROM users WHERE name = ? COLLATE NOCASE AND id != ?' ).bind(trimmed, id).first(); if (existing) return { error: 'A user with that name already exists.', status: 409 }; await env.DB.prepare('UPDATE users SET name = ? WHERE id = ?').bind(trimmed, id).run(); } if (email !== undefined) { const trimmed = email.trim(); if (!trimmed) return { error: 'Email is required.', status: 400 }; if (!isValidEmail(trimmed)) return { error: 'That email address doesn\'t look valid.', status: 400 }; await env.DB.prepare('UPDATE users SET email = ? WHERE id = ?').bind(trimmed, id).run(); } if (role !== undefined) { await env.DB.prepare('UPDATE users SET role = ? WHERE id = ?').bind(role, id).run(); } if (active !== undefined) { await env.DB.prepare('UPDATE users SET active = ? WHERE id = ?').bind(active ? 1 : 0, id).run(); } const demoted = role !== undefined && role !== user.role; const deactivated = active === false; if (demoted || deactivated) { await revokeAllSessionsForUser(env, id); } const updated = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT * FROM users WHERE id = ?').bind(id).first(); return { ok: true, ...sanitizeUser(updated) }; } async function deleteUser(env, id, actorUserId) { if (id === actorUserId) return { error: 'You cannot delete your own account.', status: 400 }; const user = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT * FROM users WHERE id = ?').bind(id).first(); if (!user) return { error: 'User not found.', status: 404 }; if (user.role === 'admin' && user.active) { const remaining = await countActiveAdmins(env, id); if (remaining < 1) return { error: 'Cannot delete the last active admin.', status: 400 }; } await revokeAllSessionsForUser(env, id); await env.DB.prepare('DELETE FROM reset_tokens WHERE user_id = ?').bind(id).run(); await env.DB.prepare('DELETE FROM users WHERE id = ?').bind(id).run(); return { ok: true }; } async function resetUserPin(env, id, kind) { const user = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT * FROM users WHERE id = ?').bind(id).first(); if (!user) return { error: 'User not found.', status: 404 }; if (!user.active) return { error: 'Cannot send a PIN to a deactivated account.', status: 400 }; const result = await issueAndSendPin(env, user, { kind }); return { ok: true, emailed: result.emailed, emailError: result.emailError, fallbackPin: result.emailed ? undefined : result.fallbackPin, }; } // ---- Baseline: Regions/Geography ------------------------------------- // §55 Worker batch B: listRegions now defaults to active regions only — // this is the picker every screen (budget tables, uploads, forecast) // draws from, and a merged-away region must disappear from all of them // without needing every call site to remember to filter. Pass // includeInactive: true only for the few contexts (Merge Region's own // source-region picker, the audit log) that need to see or reference a // region that was already merged away. async function listRegions(env, { includeInactive = false } = {}) { const rows = includeInactive ? await env.DB.prepare('SELECT * FROM regions ORDER BY sort_order ASC').all() : await env.DB.prepare('SELECT * FROM regions WHERE active = 1 ORDER BY sort_order ASC').all(); return rows.results || []; } async function renameRegion(env, id, name) { name = (name || '').trim(); if (!name) return { error: 'Region name is required.', status: 400 }; const region = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT * FROM regions WHERE id = ?').bind(id).first(); if (!region) return { error: 'Region not found.', status: 404 }; await env.DB.prepare('UPDATE regions SET name = ?, updated_at = ? WHERE id = ?') .bind(name, new Date().toISOString(), id).run(); return { ok: true }; } // ---- Baseline: Geography — state -> region assignment (§53) ---- // Separate on purpose from region_raw_mappings (which drives Weekly // Uploads region resolution and is untouched by this): this is a // territory reference table for the Geography screen, all 50 states + // DC hardcoded as rows, each with an editable region_id. async function listStates(env) { const rows = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT * FROM state_region_map ORDER BY state ASC').all(); return rows.results || []; } async function updateStateRegion(env, state, regionId, actorUserId) { state = (state || '').trim(); if (!state) return { error: 'State is required.', status: 400 }; const existing = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT * FROM state_region_map WHERE state = ?').bind(state).first(); if (!existing) return { error: 'State not found.', status: 404 }; const region = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT id FROM regions WHERE id = ?').bind(regionId).first(); if (!region) return { error: 'Region not found.', status: 404 }; await env.DB.prepare('UPDATE state_region_map SET region_id = ?, updated_by = ?, updated_at = ? WHERE state = ?') .bind(regionId, actorUserId, new Date().toISOString(), state).run(); return { ok: true }; } // ---- Baseline: Merge Region (§55.3, Worker batch B, 2026-09-04) ------ // // Standalone workflow, runnable anytime — NOT gated by the Setup // Baseline wizard's annual lock/unlock cadence. Preview-then-confirm- // then-atomic-execute: previewRegionMerge never writes anything, it only // counts what a merge WOULD touch, so the frontend can show the admin a // real number ("This will move 14 budget rows, 3,204 bookings rows...") // before they commit. executeRegionMerge is the only function that // writes, and it does so as a single D1 batch() call — all-or-nothing, // no partial-merge state ever visible. // // The source region is never hard-deleted. Every table below still // references source_region_id in its historical rows (budgets, weekly // uploads, region_raw_mappings) — reassigning those rows to // target_region_id keeps every existing report correct going forward, // while region_merges' own audit row (which itself stores // source_region_id) preserves exactly which region a given historical // number originally belonged to, if that's ever needed. `regions.active` // is what actually removes the merged-away region from every active // picker (listRegions above) without erasing its identity. // // Tables reassigned on merge, and why each one is (or isn't) included: // - revenue_budgets, revenue_budgets_monthly, sales_budgets: budget // rows are keyed by (region_id, line_of_business, fiscal_year[, // month]) with a UNIQUE constraint — if the target region ALREADY // has a budget row for the same (LOB, year[, month]), a naive UPDATE // region_id would collide. Handled explicitly below (see // mergeBudgetTableRows): colliding rows have their amounts SUMMED // into the target's existing row, then the source row is deleted, // rather than either silently overwriting or throwing on conflict. // - bookings_fact, backlog_fact, proposal_pipeline_fact, // proposal_volume_fact: plain reassignment, no unique constraint on // region_id in these append-only fact tables, so a simple UPDATE is // safe and correct. // - state_region_map: any state currently pointing at the source // region is repointed to the target, so Geography stays consistent // (a state can't be assigned to a now-inactive region). // - region_raw_mappings: any raw Salesforce region string mapped to // the source is repointed to the target, so future Weekly Uploads // resolve directly to the surviving region. // NOT reassigned: nothing in region_merges itself (that's the audit // trail, and it should keep recording source_region_id as it truly was // at merge time, not get rewritten by a later merge). const MERGEABLE_TABLES = [ { table: 'bookings_fact', column: 'region_id' }, { table: 'backlog_fact', column: 'region_id' }, { table: 'proposal_pipeline_fact', column: 'region_id' }, { table: 'proposal_volume_fact', column: 'region_id' }, { table: 'state_region_map', column: 'region_id' }, { table: 'region_raw_mappings', column: 'region_id' }, ]; // Budget tables need collision-aware merge logic (see comment above), // not the plain UPDATE the tables above use — handled separately. const MERGEABLE_BUDGET_TABLES = [ { table: 'revenue_budgets', keyColumns: ['line_of_business', 'fiscal_year'] }, { table: 'revenue_budgets_monthly', keyColumns: ['line_of_business', 'fiscal_year', 'month'] }, { table: 'sales_budgets', keyColumns: ['line_of_business', 'fiscal_year'] }, ]; async function validateMergePair(env, sourceRegionId, targetRegionId) { if (!sourceRegionId || !targetRegionId) return { error: 'Both a source and target region are required.', status: 400 }; if (sourceRegionId === targetRegionId) return { error: 'Source and target must be different regions.', status: 400 }; const source = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT * FROM regions WHERE id = ?').bind(sourceRegionId).first(); if (!source) return { error: 'Source region not found.', status: 404 }; if (!source.active) return { error: 'Source region is already merged/inactive.', status: 400 }; const target = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT * FROM regions WHERE id = ?').bind(targetRegionId).first(); if (!target) return { error: 'Target region not found.', status: 404 }; if (!target.active) return { error: 'Target region is inactive. Choose an active target.', status: 400 }; return { ok: true, source, target }; } // Row counts only, no writes. Also flags budget-row collisions (rows // that will be summed rather than moved outright) so the admin sees that // distinction before confirming, not just a raw total. async function previewRegionMerge(env, sourceRegionId, targetRegionId) { const validation = await validateMergePair(env, sourceRegionId, targetRegionId); if (validation.error) return validation; const { source, target } = validation; const counts = {}; for (const { table, column } of MERGEABLE_TABLES) { const row = await env.DB.prepare(`SELECT COUNT(*) AS n FROM ${table} WHERE ${column} = ?`).bind(sourceRegionId).first(); counts[table] = row ? row.n : 0; } const budgetCounts = {}; for (const { table, keyColumns } of MERGEABLE_BUDGET_TABLES) { const sourceRows = (await env.DB.prepare(`SELECT * FROM ${table} WHERE region_id = ?`).bind(sourceRegionId).all()).results || []; let willMove = 0, willMergeIntoExisting = 0; for (const row of sourceRows) { const whereParts = keyColumns.map(c => `${c} = ?`).join(' AND '); const existing = await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT id FROM ${table} WHERE region_id = ? AND ${whereParts}` ).bind(targetRegionId, ...keyColumns.map(c => row[c])).first(); if (existing) willMergeIntoExisting++; else willMove++; } budgetCounts[table] = { total: sourceRows.length, willMove, willMergeIntoExisting }; } return { ok: true, sourceRegion: { id: source.id, name: source.name }, targetRegion: { id: target.id, name: target.name }, rowCounts: counts, budgetRowCounts: budgetCounts, }; } // Collision-aware budget reassignment for one table: rows with no // existing target-side match are moved outright (region_id UPDATE, one // row at a time since each may need a different fate); rows that collide // with an existing target row have their amount summed into that // existing row, then the source row is deleted. Returns per-row // statements to append to the caller's atomic batch — this function // itself does not call .run(), so it composes cleanly into // executeRegionMerge's single batch() call. async function buildBudgetMergeStatements(env, table, keyColumns, sourceRegionId, targetRegionId) { const sourceRows = (await env.DB.prepare(`SELECT * FROM ${table} WHERE region_id = ?`).bind(sourceRegionId).all()).results || []; const stmts = []; const now = new Date().toISOString(); for (const row of sourceRows) { const whereParts = keyColumns.map(c => `${c} = ?`).join(' AND '); const existing = await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT * FROM ${table} WHERE region_id = ? AND ${whereParts}` ).bind(targetRegionId, ...keyColumns.map(c => row[c])).first(); if (existing) { const summedAmount = (existing.amount || 0) + (row.amount || 0); stmts.push(env.DB.prepare(`UPDATE ${table} SET amount = ?, updated_at = ? WHERE id = ?`).bind(summedAmount, now, existing.id)); stmts.push(env.DB.prepare(`DELETE FROM ${table} WHERE id = ?`).bind(row.id)); } else { stmts.push(env.DB.prepare(`UPDATE ${table} SET region_id = ?, updated_at = ? WHERE id = ?`).bind(targetRegionId, now, row.id)); } } return stmts; } // The only function in this section that writes. Builds every // reassignment/collision-merge statement plus the source-region // deactivation and the audit-log insert, then runs them all as one D1 // batch() call — atomic, all-or-nothing, matching the schema's own // region_merges design intent (a single merged_at timestamp, one // rows_affected snapshot). // newTargetName (Open Items 4(c), 2026-09-11: "Geography tab should // allow me to merge regions and give that region a new name as well") // renames the TARGET region as part of the same atomic batch, so the // merged region can end up with a brand-new name instead of only ever // keeping whichever existing region's name it was merged into — merge // and rename land together or not at all, same all-or-nothing guarantee // as the rest of this function. Optional: omitted or blank leaves the // target region's name untouched, the prior (and still fully supported) // behavior. async function executeRegionMerge(env, sourceRegionId, targetRegionId, actorUserId, newTargetName) { const validation = await validateMergePair(env, sourceRegionId, targetRegionId); if (validation.error) return validation; const { source, target } = validation; const trimmedNewName = (newTargetName || '').trim(); const now = new Date().toISOString(); const stmts = []; const rowsAffected = {}; for (const { table, column } of MERGEABLE_TABLES) { const row = await env.DB.prepare(`SELECT COUNT(*) AS n FROM ${table} WHERE ${column} = ?`).bind(sourceRegionId).first(); rowsAffected[table] = row ? row.n : 0; stmts.push(env.DB.prepare(`UPDATE ${table} SET ${column} = ? WHERE ${column} = ?`).bind(targetRegionId, sourceRegionId)); } for (const { table, keyColumns } of MERGEABLE_BUDGET_TABLES) { const budgetStmts = await buildBudgetMergeStatements(env, table, keyColumns, sourceRegionId, targetRegionId); rowsAffected[table] = budgetStmts.length; // includes both moved and merged rows stmts.push(...budgetStmts); } // Soft-delete the source region — never a hard DELETE, so its id stays // valid for every historical row still referencing it (including this // merge's own audit row below) and for region_merges rows from any // earlier merge that named it as a target. stmts.push(env.DB.prepare(`UPDATE regions SET active = 0, updated_at = ? WHERE id = ?`).bind(now, sourceRegionId)); if (trimmedNewName && trimmedNewName !== target.name) { stmts.push(env.DB.prepare(`UPDATE regions SET name = ?, updated_at = ? WHERE id = ?`).bind(trimmedNewName, now, targetRegionId)); } const mergeId = randomToken(16); stmts.push(env.DB.prepare( `INSERT INTO region_merges (id, source_region_id, target_region_id, merged_by, merged_at, rows_affected) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)` ).bind(mergeId, sourceRegionId, targetRegionId, actorUserId, now, JSON.stringify(rowsAffected))); await env.DB.batch(stmts); return { ok: true, mergeId, sourceRegion: { id: source.id, name: source.name }, targetRegion: { id: target.id, name: trimmedNewName && trimmedNewName !== target.name ? trimmedNewName : target.name }, rowsAffected, }; } async function listRegionMerges(env) { const rows = await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT rm.*, s.name AS source_region_name, t.name AS target_region_name, u.name AS merged_by_name FROM region_merges rm LEFT JOIN regions s ON s.id = rm.source_region_id LEFT JOIN regions t ON t.id = rm.target_region_id LEFT JOIN users u ON u.id = rm.merged_by ORDER BY rm.merged_at DESC LIMIT 100` ).all(); return (rows.results || []).map(r => ({ ...r, rows_affected: r.rows_affected ? JSON.parse(r.rows_affected) : {}, })); } // ---- Baseline: Service Alignment (§55.5, Worker batch B, 2026-09-04) -- // // Maps a Service Family/Service Name to a Line of Business, effective as // of a given fiscal year — e.g. QCE under Program Advisory reclassified // to Inspections starting FY2027. Natural key is // (service_family, service_name, effective_fiscal_year), deliberately // WITHOUT line_of_business: LOB is the value being assigned to a // service, not part of what identifies the service/year combination — if // LOB were part of the key, the same service could be assigned to two // different LOBs in the same year with no conflict, which defeats the // point of the table (self-caught before writing schema.sql, not a brief // requirement). // // The `asOf`-style "as reported" vs. "as currently defined" toggle // (continuity brief §55.5) is NOT implemented here — this batch is CRUD // on the mapping table only. That toggle is query-time logic for // whichever screen consumes this table to group historical bookings/ // revenue by LOB (most likely Forecast), and doesn't block having a // working mapping table with real admin-editable rows first. async function listServiceAlignments(env, { effectiveFiscalYear } = {}) { const rows = effectiveFiscalYear ? await env.DB.prepare('SELECT * FROM service_alignment WHERE effective_fiscal_year = ? ORDER BY service_family ASC, service_name ASC') .bind(effectiveFiscalYear).all() : await env.DB.prepare('SELECT * FROM service_alignment ORDER BY effective_fiscal_year DESC, service_family ASC, service_name ASC').all(); return rows.results || []; } async function upsertServiceAlignment(env, { serviceFamily, serviceName, lineOfBusiness, effectiveFiscalYear }, actorUserId) { serviceFamily = (serviceFamily || '').trim(); serviceName = (serviceName || '').trim() || null; // nullable: some alignments are family-level, not service-level if (!serviceFamily) return { error: 'Service Family is required.', status: 400 }; if (!VALID_LOBS.includes(lineOfBusiness)) return { error: 'Invalid line of business.', status: 400 }; if (!isValidFiscalYear(effectiveFiscalYear)) return { error: 'Invalid effective fiscal year.', status: 400 }; const id = randomToken(16); const now = new Date().toISOString(); // service_name is part of the UNIQUE constraint (service_family, // service_name, effective_fiscal_year); SQLite treats NULL as distinct // from every other NULL in a UNIQUE index, so ON CONFLICT would not // fire correctly for family-level rows (serviceName === null) — two // separate INSERTs for the same family/year with a null service_name // would both succeed instead of the second one updating the first. // Handled explicitly: look up any existing row matching on the actual // key (NULL-safe via IS) before deciding INSERT vs UPDATE. const existing = serviceName === null ? await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT id FROM service_alignment WHERE service_family = ? AND service_name IS NULL AND effective_fiscal_year = ?` ).bind(serviceFamily, effectiveFiscalYear).first() : await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT id FROM service_alignment WHERE service_family = ? AND service_name = ? AND effective_fiscal_year = ?` ).bind(serviceFamily, serviceName, effectiveFiscalYear).first(); if (existing) { await env.DB.prepare( `UPDATE service_alignment SET line_of_business = ?, updated_by = ?, updated_at = ? WHERE id = ?` ).bind(lineOfBusiness, actorUserId, now, existing.id).run(); } else { await env.DB.prepare( `INSERT INTO service_alignment (id, service_family, service_name, line_of_business, effective_fiscal_year, updated_by, updated_at) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)` ).bind(id, serviceFamily, serviceName, lineOfBusiness, effectiveFiscalYear, actorUserId, now).run(); } return { ok: true }; } async function deleteServiceAlignment(env, id) { const existing = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT id FROM service_alignment WHERE id = ?').bind(id).first(); if (!existing) return { error: 'Service alignment not found.', status: 404 }; await env.DB.prepare('DELETE FROM service_alignment WHERE id = ?').bind(id).run(); return { ok: true }; } // ---- Baseline: Revenue Budgets / Sales Budgets ----------------------- // Security review (2026-09-02): listBudgets/upsertBudgetCell take a // `table` parameter that gets interpolated into raw SQL. Every current // call site passes only a hardcoded literal, so this was never reachable // with attacker-controlled input — but the functions themselves had no // guard, so the first future route forwarding anything request-derived // into `table` would have been a direct SQL injection with nothing to // catch it. Fixed with an explicit allowlist checked before the table // name ever reaches a query string, so this stays true by construction // rather than by every future caller remembering to be careful. const VALID_BUDGET_TABLES = new Set(['revenue_budgets', 'sales_budgets']); function assertValidBudgetTable(table) { if (!VALID_BUDGET_TABLES.has(table)) { throw new Error(`Invalid budget table: ${table}`); } } async function listBudgets(env, table, fiscalYear) { assertValidBudgetTable(table); const rows = await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT * FROM ${table} WHERE fiscal_year = ?` ).bind(fiscalYear).all(); return rows.results || []; } // Real fiscal years this app deals in: current year budgeting plus a // reasonable planning horizon. Rejects garbage (-1, 0, 99999) before it // ever reaches a budget row, per the security review's data-integrity // finding — a bad year previously wrote cleanly and just went missing // from every report that filters by a real year. const MIN_FISCAL_YEAR = 2020; const MAX_FISCAL_YEAR = 2100; function isValidFiscalYear(year) { return Number.isInteger(year) && year >= MIN_FISCAL_YEAR && year <= MAX_FISCAL_YEAR; } function isValidMonth(month) { return Number.isInteger(month) && month >= 1 && month <= 12; } // Security review (2026-09-02, finding #3): createUser/updateUser only // checked that an email was non-empty. A malformed address (a typo, a // pasted name, garbage) previously saved cleanly and then silently broke // the welcome-PIN / reset-PIN email send with no validation error pointing // back at the bad input.
