Currently zero real-world // impact since service_alignment is confirmed empty in production, but // wrong the moment an admin adds a real override. const alignmentRowsForPriorYear = await listServiceAlignments(env, { effectiveFiscalYear: priorYear }); // Per-account LOB totals for the CURRENT fiscal year, this region. const accountLob = new Map(); // parent_company -> { program_advisory, design_services, inspections } for (const r of accountRows) { const lob = resolveLineOfBusiness(r.service_family, r.service_name, fiscalYear, alignmentRowsForYear); if (!lob) continue; // unrecognized service_family: excluded, not guessed at if (!accountLob.has(r.parent_company)) accountLob.set(r.parent_company, { program_advisory: 0, design_services: 0, inspections: 0 }); accountLob.get(r.parent_company)[lob] += r.total || 0; } // 1. Top 5 accounts with Inspections but zero Program Advisory ($). const zeroAttachAccounts = [...accountLob.entries()] .filter(([, v]) => v.inspections > 0 && v.program_advisory === 0) .map(([name, v]) => ({ name, inspections: v.inspections })) .sort((a, b) => b.inspections - a.inspections) .slice(0, 5); // 2. Inspection clients with Program Advisory attach %: of accounts // buying Inspections this year, what share also buy Program Advisory. const inspectionAccounts = [...accountLob.entries()].filter(([, v]) => v.inspections > 0); const inspectionWithAdvisory = inspectionAccounts.filter(([, v]) => v.program_advisory > 0); const inspectionAttachPct = inspectionAccounts.length > 0 ? (inspectionWithAdvisory.length / inspectionAccounts.length) * 100 : null; // 3. Renewal / retention rate (Inspections): accounts buying Inspections // in BOTH priorYear and fiscalYear, ÷ accounts buying Inspections in // priorYear — same shape as the whole-account Client Retention Rate // above, scoped to Inspections specifically. const priorAccountLob = new Map(); for (const r of priorAccountRows) { const lob = resolveLineOfBusiness(r.service_family, r.service_name, priorYear, alignmentRowsForPriorYear); if (!lob) continue; if (!priorAccountLob.has(r.parent_company)) priorAccountLob.set(r.parent_company, { program_advisory: 0, design_services: 0, inspections: 0 }); priorAccountLob.get(r.parent_company)[lob] += r.total || 0; } const priorInspectionAccountNames = new Set([...priorAccountLob.entries()].filter(([, v]) => v.inspections > 0).map(([name]) => name)); const currentInspectionAccountNames = new Set(inspectionAccounts.map(([name]) => name)); const inspectionRetainedCount = [...priorInspectionAccountNames].filter(n => currentInspectionAccountNames.has(n)).length; const inspectionRetentionRatePct = priorInspectionAccountNames.size > 0 ? (inspectionRetainedCount / priorInspectionAccountNames.size) * 100 : null; // 4. Softest renewal segments: same retention logic as #3, grouped by // backlog_fact.vertical_end_market instead of by account. A segment's // "prior" accounts are backlog_fact rows for that market whose // parent_customer_name also appears among priorInspectionAccountNames // (backlog_fact and bookings_fact key accounts by different column // names — parent_customer_name vs. parent_company — confirmed against // sales/schema.sql, not assumed to match by coincidence). const backlogMarketRows = (await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT vertical_end_market, parent_customer_name FROM backlog_fact WHERE region_id = ? AND vertical_end_market IS NOT NULL AND vertical_end_market != '' AND parent_customer_name IS NOT NULL AND parent_customer_name != ''` ).bind(regionId).all()).results || []; const marketPriorAccounts = new Map(); // market -> Set(account names, prior-year Inspections buyers only) const marketAllAccounts = new Map(); // market -> Set(account names seen in backlog at all, for the denominator) for (const r of backlogMarketRows) { if (!marketAllAccounts.has(r.vertical_end_market)) marketAllAccounts.set(r.vertical_end_market, new Set()); marketAllAccounts.get(r.vertical_end_market).add(r.parent_customer_name); if (priorInspectionAccountNames.has(r.parent_customer_name)) { if (!marketPriorAccounts.has(r.vertical_end_market)) marketPriorAccounts.set(r.vertical_end_market, new Set()); marketPriorAccounts.get(r.vertical_end_market).add(r.parent_customer_name); } } const softestRenewalSegments = [...marketPriorAccounts.entries()] .map(([market, priorSet]) => { const retained = [...priorSet].filter(n => currentInspectionAccountNames.has(n)).length; return { market, priorCount: priorSet.size, retainedCount: retained, retentionPct: priorSet.size > 0 ? (retained / priorSet.size) * 100 : null }; }) .filter(s => s.priorCount >= 2) // exclude single-account segments: one lost account reading as "0% retention" is noise, not a real trend .sort((a, b) => a.retentionPct - b.retentionPct) .slice(0, 5); const lineOfBusinessDetail = { zeroAttachAccounts, inspectionAttach: { pct: inspectionAttachPct, withAdvisoryCount: inspectionWithAdvisory.length, totalInspectionAccounts: inspectionAccounts.length }, inspectionRetention: { pct: inspectionRetentionRatePct, retainedCount: inspectionRetainedCount, priorCount: priorInspectionAccountNames.size }, softestRenewalSegments, }; // ---- Next $1M, Best Opportunity ---- // Scores every family on 3 real signals: whitespace $ (region total in // named accounts missing this family), gap vs. company-wide benchmark // (region $ vs. what the same family does company-wide, normalized), // and YoY trend direction (region total this year vs. prior year). // Retention is a GATE, not a 4th scored signal (confirmed approach, // continuity brief): a family otherwise ranked 1st is downgraded one // rank if region retention is below the company benchmark, surfaced // in the UI rather than silently applied. const accountsMissingFamily = new Map(); // family key -> [{name, total}] for (const f of ACCOUNT_SERVICE_FAMILIES) accountsMissingFamily.set(f.key, []); for (const a of accountsRanked) { for (const f of ACCOUNT_SERVICE_FAMILIES) { if (!a.families.has(f.key)) accountsMissingFamily.get(f.key).push({ name: a.name, total: a.total }); } } const familyScores = ACCOUNT_SERVICE_FAMILIES.map(f => { const missing = accountsMissingFamily.get(f.key).sort((a, b) => b.total - a.total); // Whitespace $ = a real "typical account's spend in this family" // (company-wide average, see companyFamilyAvgPerAccount above) times // the number of region accounts missing it — NOT the sum of those // accounts' own whole-account revenue (that was the bug: an account's // total revenue across every service it buys has nothing to do with // what it would likely spend on a family it doesn't buy yet, and // summing it per family duplicated the same figure across every // family missing from the same account set). const whitespace = missing.length * (companyFamilyAvgPerAccount[f.key] || 0); const regionVal = regionFamilyTotals[f.key] || 0; const companyVal = companyFamilyTotals[f.key] || 0; const benchmarkGapPct = companyVal > 0 ? ((regionVal - companyVal) / companyVal) * 100 : null; const priorVal = priorRegionFamilyTotals[f.key] || 0; const yoyPct = priorVal > 0 ? ((regionVal - priorVal) / priorVal) * 100 : null; return { key: f.key, label: f.label, whitespace, whitespaceAccountCount: missing.length, topWhitespaceAccounts: missing.slice(0, 3).map(a => a.name), benchmarkGapPct, yoyPct, }; }); // Normalize each signal to 0-1 and blend equally, then rank. Simple, // visible weighting (not a black box) — the raw table is shown // alongside the rank so the "why" is never hidden. const maxWhitespace = Math.max(1, ...familyScores.map(f => f.whitespace)); const scored = familyScores.map(f => { const whitespaceScore = f.whitespace / maxWhitespace; const gapScore = f.benchmarkGapPct === null ? 0.5 : Math.max(0, Math.min(1, (0 - f.benchmarkGapPct) / 100 + 0.5)); const trendScore = f.yoyPct === null ? 0.5 : Math.max(0, Math.min(1, f.yoyPct / 100 + 0.5)); const blended = whitespaceScore * 0.5 + gapScore * 0.3 + trendScore * 0.2; return { ...f, blended }; }).sort((a, b) => b.blended - a.blended); const retentionGateTriggered = retentionRatePct !== null && companyRetentionRatePct !== null && retentionRatePct < companyRetentionRatePct; if (retentionGateTriggered && scored.length > 1) { // Move the top-ranked family down one spot rather than dropping it, // per the "gate, not a silent override" design. [scored[0], scored[1]] = [scored[1], scored[0]]; } const nextMillion = scored.slice(0, 3).map((f, i) => ({ ...f, rank: i + 1 })); // ---- Where to Focus (generated, editable in the UI before printing) ---- const top = nextMillion[0]; const focusAccounts = top ? top.topWhitespaceAccounts.slice(0, 2) : []; let whereToFocus = null; if (top) { const trendPhrase = top.yoyPct === null ? 'trend not yet available' : top.yoyPct >= 0 ? `trending up ${top.yoyPct.toFixed(0)}%` : `trending down ${Math.abs(top.yoyPct).toFixed(0)}%`; const benchmarkPhrase = top.benchmarkGapPct === null ? 'no company-wide benchmark yet' : top.benchmarkGapPct < 0 ? `${Math.abs(top.benchmarkGapPct).toFixed(0)}% below its company-wide benchmark` : `${top.benchmarkGapPct.toFixed(0)}% above its company-wide benchmark`; const acctPhrase = focusAccounts.length > 0 ? `Prioritize ${focusAccounts.join(' and ')} first, both missing ${top.label} and among the region's largest whitespace accounts.` : `No named accounts currently missing ${top.label}.`; const retentionPhrase = retentionRatePct === null ? '' : ` Retention is holding at ${retentionRatePct.toFixed(0)}%${companyRetentionRatePct !== null ? `, vs. ${companyRetentionRatePct.toFixed(0)}% company-wide` : ''}.`; whereToFocus = `${top.label} is the region's top-ranked opportunity: real whitespace in ${top.whitespaceAccountCount} named account${top.whitespaceAccountCount === 1 ? '' : 's'}, ${benchmarkPhrase}, and ${trendPhrase}. ${acctPhrase}${retentionPhrase}`; } return { ...base, accountConcentration: concentration, lowestSellingServices, salesVolumeLeaderboard: { topAccountsByVolume, topServicesByVolume }, oneServiceOnlyAccounts, proposalWinRate: { available: proposalWinRateAvailable, services: proposalWinRate }, retention: { regionPct: retentionRatePct, companyPct: companyRetentionRatePct }, accountTenureMix, nextMillion, retentionGateTriggered, whereToFocus, lineOfBusinessDetail, }; } // ---- Revenue Bridge (2026-09-06), per-region YTD Actuals to Expected -- // // Real build-up methodology confirmed with Misty directly (see continuity // brief §19 for the original scoping, this segment for the real-data // verification pass that resolved every open question below against // live schema rather than guessing): // // 1. YTD Actuals: billed_fact, this region, months already elapsed. // 2. Inspections Run Rate: billed_fact filtered to Inspections, reusing // computeBlendedForecast() exactly as Forecast already does, no new // formula. Inspections has no backlog concept, so it never appears in // steps 3/4 below, and it's deliberately excluded from step 6 to avoid // double-counting it (it's already fully represented via run rate). // 3/4. Program Advisory / Design Services Backlog: backlog_fact.contract_ // balance for this region, same query already used by // getRegionsScreen's Backlog by Line of Business section. // 5. Weighted Pipeline: proposal_pipeline_fact.total_vda_fees x // probability_pct, this region, ALL LOBs combined, confirmed with // Misty ("One combined step") since this table has no service/LOB // column at all (opportunity grain only) and can't be split without a // new column and a data-entry change on the Salesforce side. // 6. Proposal Volume x Win Rate: Program Advisory and Design Services // only (Inspections excluded per #2 above). Volume base is ALL // proposal_volume_fact rows issued in the trailing 12 months for that // region/LOB regardless of stage (Misty's direct call: "look at // proposal volume and then won proposals to get the hit rate" — this // represents the real rate of new activity being created, not a // filtered "still open" subset), multiplied by that same region/LOB's // real win rate (Closed Won / (Closed Won + Closed Lost)) over the // same trailing 12 months. Labeled "Win Rate" in the UI, not // "Conversion Rate", per Misty's direct correction. // 7. Expected Revenue: sum of the six components above. // // Reconciliation check (added beyond the original §19 spec, per this // segment's McKinsey-style review): Expected Revenue is compared against // this region's stated annual Revenue Budget (listRevenueBudgetsAnnualDerived, // the same real, monthly-derived figure Baseline already treats as // authoritative since §78), and the variance is surfaced by name rather // than silently absorbed into one of the bars above. This is the // discipline that makes the bridge an accountability tool rather than // just a chart. // regionId === null means company-wide (All Regions), consistent with // how getForecastMonthPace/getForecastRegionalDrivers already report // company-wide figures with no region filter at all. A real region id // scopes every step to that region only, matching the original per- // region spec (§19). Executive Summary uses the company-wide form for // its own Revenue Bridge card; a future per-region bridge view (if ever // added) would reuse this same function with a real regionId. // Revenue Bridge stage-default probability setting (2026-09-08, §91/§97 // scoped feature). Replaces the auto-computed dollarWeightedWinRate // substitution below with an admin-adjustable, persisted value, default // 40% (Misty: "roughly splits the difference"), scoped to fiscal_year. // Fallback is the literal 40, not the historical win rate, per the // confirmed table default — no row yet for a fiscal year just means // nobody has moved the slider there yet. async function getRegionPipelineDefaultProbability(env, fiscalYear) { const row = await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT probability_pct, updated_by, updated_at FROM pipeline_default_probability_settings WHERE fiscal_year = ?` ).bind(fiscalYear).first(); return { probabilityPct: row ? row.probability_pct : 40, updatedBy: row ? row.updated_by : null, updatedAt: row ? row.updated_at : null, }; } async function upsertRegionPipelineDefaultProbability(env, { fiscalYear, probabilityPct }, actorUserId) { if (!isValidFiscalYear(fiscalYear)) return { error: 'Invalid fiscal year.', status: 400 }; const pct = Number(probabilityPct); if (!Number.isFinite(pct) || pct < 0 || pct > 100) return { error: 'Probability must be a number between 0 and 100.', status: 400 }; await env.DB.prepare( `INSERT INTO pipeline_default_probability_settings (fiscal_year, probability_pct, updated_by, updated_at) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?) ON CONFLICT(fiscal_year) DO UPDATE SET probability_pct = excluded.probability_pct, updated_by = excluded.updated_by, updated_at = excluded.updated_at` ).bind(fiscalYear, pct, actorUserId, new Date().toISOString()).run(); return { ok: true }; } async function getRevenueBridge(env, fiscalYear, regionId) { let region = null; if (regionId) { region = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT id, name FROM regions WHERE id = ?').bind(regionId).first(); if (!region) return { error: 'Region not found.', status: 404 }; } const asOf = await resolveForecastAsOf(env); // ---- Step 1: YTD Actuals (billed_fact, months already elapsed) ---- const billedRows = (await env.DB.prepare( regionId ? `SELECT revenue_type, month, SUM(amount) AS total FROM billed_fact WHERE region_id = ? AND fiscal_year = ? GROUP BY revenue_type, month` : `SELECT revenue_type, month, SUM(amount) AS total FROM billed_fact WHERE fiscal_year = ? GROUP BY revenue_type, month` ).bind(...(regionId ? [regionId, fiscalYear] : [fiscalYear])).all()).results || []; const billedAvailable = billedRows.length > 0; const monthlyByLob = { program_advisory: new Map(), design_services: new Map(), inspections: new Map() }; let billedUnmapped = 0; for (const r of billedRows) { const lob = mapRevenueTypeToLob(r.revenue_type); if (!lob) { billedUnmapped += r.total || 0; continue; } monthlyByLob[lob].set(r.month, (monthlyByLob[lob].get(r.month) || 0) + (r.total || 0)); } const monthlyAllLob = new Map(); for (const lobKey of Object.keys(monthlyByLob)) { for (const [month, amt] of monthlyByLob[lobKey].entries()) { monthlyAllLob.set(month, (monthlyAllLob.get(month) || 0) + amt); } } // YTD Actuals = every fully-elapsed prior month's total plus the // current month's actuals-to-date (NOT blended — this step is what has // really landed so far, the blended projection only enters via step 2's // Inspections run rate and is never applied to the other two LOBs since // they contribute via backlog/pipeline instead, not a billed-fact // extrapolation). let ytdActuals = 0; for (const [month, amt] of monthlyAllLob.entries()) { if (month <= asOf.month) ytdActuals += amt; } // ---- Step 2: Inspections Run Rate (real blended forecast, reused) ---- // Fixed 2026-09-06 (Misty: "still way too high. Inspection run rate // seems high too as a side note" + "I think the inspections budget for // the whole year was $60M"). Bug: this used to be // inspectionsForecast.totalYearForecast, which is priorActualsSum + // currentMonthBlended + remainingMonthsForecast -- i.e. the WHOLE // year's Inspections total, actuals and forecast combined. But Step 1 // (YTD Actuals, above) already sums every LOB's real billed_fact // dollars through the current month, Inspections included. So Step 2 // was re-adding all of Inspections' YTD actuals a second time on top // of Step 1, then adding the forecasted remainder as well -- a // straightforward double-count, not a forecasting bug. Confirmed via // AskUserQuestion which of two fix shapes to use ("Only remaining // months" vs. "keep full-year total but strip Inspections out of Step // 1 instead"); Misty chose the former, the standard incremental // run-rate meaning. Step 2 is now ONLY the portion of the year not yet // reflected in Step 1's actuals: the forecasted (not-yet-actual) part // of the current month, plus the average-based projection for the // fully future months. total_year_forecast - prior_actuals_sum - // current_month_actuals collapses to exactly that. const inspectionsForecast = computeBlendedForecast(monthlyByLob.inspections, asOf); const inspectionsRunRate = inspectionsForecast.totalYearForecast - inspectionsForecast.priorActualsSum - inspectionsForecast.currentMonthActuals; // ---- Steps 3/4: Program Advisory / Design Services Backlog ---- // Same query and LOB mapping as getRegionsScreen's Backlog by Line of // Business section (contract_balance, end_date year = fiscalYear + 1). const backlogRows = (await env.DB.prepare( regionId ? `SELECT revenue_type, SUM(contract_balance) AS total FROM backlog_fact WHERE region_id = ? AND end_date IS NOT NULL AND end_date != '' AND ${END_YEAR_SQL} = ? GROUP BY revenue_type` : `SELECT revenue_type, SUM(contract_balance) AS total FROM backlog_fact WHERE end_date IS NOT NULL AND end_date != '' AND ${END_YEAR_SQL} = ? GROUP BY revenue_type` ).bind(...(regionId ? [regionId, fiscalYear + 1] : [fiscalYear + 1])).all()).results || []; let paBacklog = 0, dsBacklog = 0; for (const r of backlogRows) { const lob = mapRevenueTypeToLob(r.revenue_type); if (lob === 'program_advisory') paBacklog += r.total || 0; else if (lob === 'design_services') dsBacklog += r.total || 0; } // ---- Step 5: Weighted Pipeline (combined, table has no LOB column) ---- // Fixed 2026-09-06 (Misty: "expected revenue is ridiculously too high... // are you looking at expected award date"). The original query summed // EVERY row in proposal_pipeline_fact with a fee and probability, with // no filter on estimated_award_date and no exclusion of Closed Won/ // Closed Lost rows -- confirmed via live sample data (Misty ran the // query) that real rows include a stale 2/27/2020 estimated_award_date // and a next-fiscal-year 1/11/2027 one, so a proposal expected to close // years outside this fiscal year, or already resolved, was inflating // Expected Revenue. First fix, confirmed with Misty: only genuinely // open stages count as "pipeline" -- Closed Won is already counted in // YTD Actuals (double-counting it here would inflate the bridge twice // over), and Closed Lost isn't pipeline at all. // // Second fix, same day, after Misty's own diagnosis of the still-too- // high total: "if it has estimated award dates that have passed, it's // a good chance the team did list as lost [but never updated the // stage]." Verified this directly rather than assume it: of the open, // FY2026-award-dated rows, nearly half the weighted value ($36.08M of // $75.26M, 2,985 of 4,566 rows) had an estimated_award_date already in // the past -- real, material stale-pipeline risk, not a theoretical // edge case. Misty's confirmed rule: exclude a row only once its // estimated_award_date is more than 60 days past asOf (this app's // real data-driven "today", from resolveForecastAsOf) -- close enough // to still be a live, just-slow-to-update deal is kept; anything // further overdue with no stage update is dropped as untrustworthy. // This 60-day cutoff is a rolling window against asOf, not the // calendar year, so a row ages out of the bridge as time passes // unless the team corrects its date or stage -- "it will change // monthly," exactly as Misty expected and wanted. // // Day-level precision is needed for a 60-day window (unlike the // year/month-only comparisons used elsewhere in this bridge), so // rather than force fragile day-arithmetic SQL onto a non-standard // M/D/YYYY text column, the open FY2026 rows are pulled with their // raw estimated_award_date and filtered/summed in JS with a real // Date object -- easier to get right and easier to audit than nested // SUBSTR/INSTR SQL for day-level date math. const pipelineRows = (await env.DB.prepare( regionId ? `SELECT total_vda_fees, probability_pct, estimated_award_date, stage FROM proposal_pipeline_fact WHERE region_id = ? AND total_vda_fees IS NOT NULL AND probability_pct IS NOT NULL AND estimated_award_date IS NOT NULL AND estimated_award_date != '' AND ${ESTIMATED_AWARD_YEAR_SQL} = ? AND stage NOT IN ('Closed Won', 'Closed Lost')` : `SELECT total_vda_fees, probability_pct, estimated_award_date, stage FROM proposal_pipeline_fact WHERE total_vda_fees IS NOT NULL AND probability_pct IS NOT NULL AND estimated_award_date IS NOT NULL AND estimated_award_date != '' AND ${ESTIMATED_AWARD_YEAR_SQL} = ? AND stage NOT IN ('Closed Won', 'Closed Lost')` ).bind(...(regionId ? [regionId, fiscalYear] : [fiscalYear])).all()).results || []; // Stage-default probability override, added 2026-09-06 (Misty, live // screenshot: "still too high. The weighted pipeline value is off the // chart"). Investigated with live queries rather than assumed: 4,778 of // 4,868 Negotiation/Review rows (98%) and 338 of 342 Proposal/Price // Quote rows (99%) carry the EXACT SAME probability_pct value, 75 -- // confirmed via GROUP BY probability_pct that this is a Salesforce // stage-default auto-fill, not 5,000+ individually underwritten // judgment calls, since real rep-entered variation (the other ~90-4 // rows at 5/10/25/33/45/50/55/90) exists in both stages alongside it. // Treating a flat, unassessed default as if it were real per-deal // confidence overstated Weighted Pipeline. // // Fix, confirmed via AskUserQuestion against three options (discount // further / keep as-is / flag only): replace the stage-default value // with VDA's own real dollar-weighted historical win rate instead of // an invented discount. Computed live below as SUM(fees where Closed // Won) / SUM(fees where Closed Won or Closed Lost) across the whole // table -- 76.1% at the time of this fix (vs. 89.5% by deal COUNT; // Misty confirmed dollar-weighted is the right basis since the bridge // forecasts dollars). This is a live query, not a hardcoded literal, // so it drifts automatically as more deals close. // // Scope, confirmed directly with Misty: applies only to rows AT // EXACTLY 75% in Negotiation/Review or Proposal/Price Quote (the two // stages confirmed via live query to show the same near-universal // single-default pattern). On Hold/Prospecting/Qualification were // checked at a glance (47%/25%/29% averages, far fewer rows, more // spread) and left alone -- lower dollar impact and not confirmed as // the same default-artifact pattern. Any row with a real, different // rep-entered probability (in any stage) is untouched, including the // ~90/~4 non-75% rows inside these same two stages. // // 2026-09-08 (§91/§97): the SUBSTITUTE value is now the admin-set, // persisted pipeline_default_probability_settings percentage (default // 40, via the new Revenue Bridge slider) instead of the auto-computed // dollarWeightedWinRate below. dollarWeightedWinRate is still computed // and returned, but only as reference context shown next to the // slider (VDA's real historical dollar-weighted win rate), never // applied automatically. const winRateRow = await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT SUM(CASE WHEN stage = 'Closed Won' THEN total_vda_fees ELSE 0 END) AS won_fees, SUM(CASE WHEN stage IN ('Closed Won', 'Closed Lost') THEN total_vda_fees ELSE 0 END) AS closed_fees FROM proposal_pipeline_fact WHERE total_vda_fees IS NOT NULL` ).first(); const historicalDollarWeightedWinRatePct = (winRateRow && winRateRow.closed_fees > 0) ? (winRateRow.won_fees / winRateRow.closed_fees) * 100 : null; const defaultProbabilitySetting = await getRegionPipelineDefaultProbability(env, fiscalYear); const STAGE_DEFAULT_PROBABILITY = 75; const STAGE_DEFAULT_OVERRIDE_STAGES = new Set(['Negotiation/Review', 'Proposal/Price Quote']); const PIPELINE_PAST_DUE_GRACE_DAYS = 60; const asOfDateForPipeline = new Date(Date.UTC(asOf.year, asOf.month - 1, asOf.day)); let weightedPipeline = 0; let pipelineStaleExcludedCount = 0, pipelineStaleExcludedValue = 0; let pipelineDefaultOverriddenCount = 0, pipelineDefaultOverriddenFees = 0; for (const r of pipelineRows) { const parts = r.estimated_award_date.split('/'); if (parts.length !== 3) continue; const m = parseInt(parts[0], 10), d = parseInt(parts[1], 10), y = parseInt(parts[2], 10); if (!m || !d || !y) continue; const awardDate = new Date(Date.UTC(y, m - 1, d)); const daysPastDue = (asOfDateForPipeline - awardDate) / (24 * 60 * 60 * 1000); let probability = r.probability_pct; const isStageDefault = probability === STAGE_DEFAULT_PROBABILITY && STAGE_DEFAULT_OVERRIDE_STAGES.has(r.stage); if (isStageDefault) { probability = defaultProbabilitySetting.probabilityPct; pipelineDefaultOverriddenCount += 1; pipelineDefaultOverriddenFees += r.total_vda_fees || 0; } const contribution = r.total_vda_fees * probability / 100.0; if (daysPastDue > PIPELINE_PAST_DUE_GRACE_DAYS) { pipelineStaleExcludedCount += 1; pipelineStaleExcludedValue += contribution; continue; } weightedPipeline += contribution; } // ---- Step 6: Proposal Volume x Win Rate (PA + DS only) ---- // Fixed 2026-09-06, same live-testing pass as the Step 5 fix (Misty: // "still way too high... budget expectation is $137M, you're // projecting almost 3x that"). Root cause here was a real design bug, // not another date-format bug: volumeBase originally summed EVERY // proposal line issued in the trailing 12 months regardless of stage, // then multiplied that whole population by a win rate and added the // result as NEW Expected Revenue. But "every proposal issued in the // last 12 months" is overwhelmingly proposals that have ALREADY // resolved -- either already won (already counted in Step 1 YTD // Actuals or Steps 3/4 Backlog) or already lost (never real future // revenue at all) -- so multiplying that whole base by a win rate and // adding it on top of the other steps was double- and triple-counting // most of the trailing year's proposal activity. Confirmed the fix // directly with Misty before writing it: the win rate itself still // needs the full resolved trailing-12-months population (Closed Won / // (Closed Won + Closed Lost)) to be a meaningful rate, but the volume // base it's applied against -- the part that represents real, // not-yet-counted future business -- is now ONLY the proposals still // in an open stage (Prospecting, Qualification, Proposal/Price Quote, // Negotiation/Review, On Hold), not the resolved ones. const asOfYm = asOf.year * 12 + asOf.month; const trailingCutoffYm = asOfYm - 12; const volumeRows = (await env.DB.prepare( regionId ? `SELECT revenue_type, stage, SUM(total_price) AS total FROM proposal_volume_fact WHERE region_id = ? AND proposal_issued_date IS NOT NULL AND proposal_issued_date != '' AND ${PROPOSAL_ISSUED_YM_SQL} > ? AND ${PROPOSAL_ISSUED_YM_SQL} <= ? GROUP BY revenue_type, stage` : `SELECT revenue_type, stage, SUM(total_price) AS total FROM proposal_volume_fact WHERE proposal_issued_date IS NOT NULL AND proposal_issued_date != '' AND ${PROPOSAL_ISSUED_YM_SQL} > ? AND ${PROPOSAL_ISSUED_YM_SQL} <= ? GROUP BY revenue_type, stage` ).bind(...(regionId ? [regionId, trailingCutoffYm, asOfYm] : [trailingCutoffYm, asOfYm])).all()).results || []; const volumeByLob = { program_advisory: { open: 0, won: 0, lost: 0 }, design_services: { open: 0, won: 0, lost: 0 } }; for (const r of volumeRows) { const lob = mapRevenueTypeToLob(r.revenue_type); if (lob !== 'program_advisory' && lob !== 'design_services') continue; if (r.stage === 'Closed Won') volumeByLob[lob].won += r.total || 0; else if (r.stage === 'Closed Lost') volumeByLob[lob].lost += r.total || 0; else volumeByLob[lob].open += r.total || 0; } const volumeAvailable = volumeRows.length > 0; // Fixed 2026-09-06 (Misty: "is there anything else getting double // counted? bridge still seems off" -- after the Step 2 fix above, // Expected Revenue was still ~2.1x the stated budget). Root cause: // proposal_pipeline_fact (Step 5, opportunity grain) and // proposal_volume_fact (Step 6, line-item grain) are two different // exports of the SAME underlying Salesforce opportunities, not two // independent business populations -- confirmed live (Misty ran the // query): 4,462 of the open proposal_pipeline_fact rows have a // matching opportunity_name in proposal_volume_fact. So an open deal // was being counted twice: once in Step 5 (real per-opportunity // probability x fee) and again in Step 6 (a share of the same dollars // via win rate). Confirmed the fix via AskUserQuestion: rather than // attempt an opportunity-name anti-join between two independently- // uploaded weekly exports (fragile -- no guarantee the same opportunity // is named identically in both files every week), Step 6's DOLLAR // CONTRIBUTION is dropped from Expected Revenue entirely. Step 5's // real per-opportunity probability is the more trustworthy open- // pipeline-value signal for PA+DS, so it remains the sole source of // that money. Step 6 still computes and returns the real Win Rate % // (Closed Won / (Closed Won + Closed Lost)) and volume base -- useful // context, kept for the UI -- it just no longer adds a dollar figure // into the bridge. function volumeTimesWinRate(lobKey) { const d = volumeByLob[lobKey]; const denom = d.won + d.lost; const winRatePct = denom > 0 ? (d.won / denom) * 100 : null; return { volumeBase: d.open, winRatePct }; } const paVolume = volumeTimesWinRate('program_advisory'); const dsVolume = volumeTimesWinRate('design_services'); // ---- Step 7: Expected Revenue ---- // proposal_volume_win_rate is NOT summed in here (see the comment on // volumeTimesWinRate above) -- its dollars overlap with Weighted // Pipeline, so including both would double-count the same open // opportunities a second time. const expectedRevenue = ytdActuals + inspectionsRunRate + paBacklog + dsBacklog + weightedPipeline; const steps = [ { key: 'ytd_actuals', label: 'YTD Actuals', kind: 'total', value: ytdActuals }, { key: 'inspections_run_rate', label: 'Inspections Run Rate', kind: 'pos', value: inspectionsRunRate }, { key: 'pa_backlog', label: 'Program Advisory Backlog', kind: 'pos', value: paBacklog }, { key: 'ds_backlog', label: 'Design Services Backlog', kind: 'pos', value: dsBacklog }, { key: 'weighted_pipeline', label: 'Weighted Pipeline', kind: 'pos', value: weightedPipeline }, { key: 'expected_revenue', label: 'Expected Revenue', kind: 'total', value: expectedRevenue }, ]; // ---- Reconciliation check vs. stated Revenue Budget ---- // regionId === null (company-wide): sums every region's derived annual // Revenue Budget rather than filtering to one, matching how every // other company-wide figure on this screen (Month Pace, etc.) is // itself an unfiltered sum across all regions. const revenueAnnualRows = await listRevenueBudgetsAnnualDerived(env, fiscalYear); const revenueBudget = revenueAnnualRows .filter(r => regionId ? r.region_id === regionId : true) .reduce((s, r) => s + (r.amount || 0), 0); const variance = expectedRevenue - revenueBudget; const variancePct = revenueBudget > 0 ? (variance / revenueBudget) * 100 : null; return { region: region ? { id: region.id, name: region.name } : { id: null, name: 'All Regions' }, fiscalYear, asOf, billedAvailable, volumeAvailable, steps, detail: { // remainingYearForecast is what Step 2 actually contributes (the // not-yet-actual portion of the year); fullYearForecast is the // whole-year Inspections total (actuals + forecast) surfaced only // for reference/debugging, never summed into Expected Revenue. inspectionsRunRate: { remainingYearForecast: inspectionsRunRate, fullYearForecast: inspectionsForecast.totalYearForecast, priorAvgPerMonth: inspectionsForecast.priorAvgPerMonth, }, paVolume, dsVolume, billedUnmapped, // Surfaced so the stale-pipeline exclusion (2026-09-06, Misty's // 60-day-past-due rule) is auditable in the app, not a silent // adjustment to Weighted Pipeline. pipelineStaleExcluded: { count: pipelineStaleExcludedCount, value: pipelineStaleExcludedValue, graceDays: PIPELINE_PAST_DUE_GRACE_DAYS }, pipelineStageDefaultOverride: { count: pipelineDefaultOverriddenCount, totalFees: pipelineDefaultOverriddenFees, defaultProbability: STAGE_DEFAULT_PROBABILITY, appliedProbabilityPct: defaultProbabilitySetting.probabilityPct, historicalDollarWeightedWinRatePct, stages: Array.from(STAGE_DEFAULT_OVERRIDE_STAGES), }, }, // Slider state (2026-09-08): the currently-effective, persisted // probability applied to stage-default rows this fiscal year, plus // who/when it was last set (null/null if nobody has ever moved it, // meaning it's on the 40% fallback). Read by the frontend to // initialize the Revenue Bridge slider from the same payload that // already loads the whole bridge. pipelineDefaultProbability: defaultProbabilitySetting, reconciliation: { expectedRevenue, revenueBudget, variance, variancePct, }, }; } // ---- Executive Summary (2026-09-06), first real build ----------------- // // Executive Summary was a disabled nav placeholder until this build // (confirmed by grep: no subpane, no PERIOD_DATA, no tiles anywhere in // the real frontend — everything with that name in the continuity brief // prior to this segment was mockup-only content that never shipped). // Investigated before writing anything, per Misty's direct instruction: // two of this screen's three natural content pieces ALREADY have real, // fully-built backend functions living in Forecast, unused by any other // screen, and are reused here rather than recomputed a second way, same // discipline as Forecast's own Revenue path reusing // listRevenueBudgetsAnnualDerived instead of a parallel query: // - Pace tiles: getForecastMonthPace (company-wide blended-forecast // pace, Sales and Revenue, vs. trailing average and vs. budget). // - Regions Below Budget Pace table: getForecastRegionalDrivers // (per-region pace with a real two-level shortfall drill-down). // The only genuinely new content is the Revenue Bridge (this segment's // build, see getRevenueBridge above, called here with regionId=null for // the company-wide form) and the Service Family chart, which reuses the // same real 9-family taxonomy (ACCOUNT_SERVICE_FAMILIES/ // mapServiceToAccountFamily) already used on Regions' Biggest Accounts // card, company-wide grain, current fiscal year, sorted by $ — chosen // over inventing a new grouping since this taxonomy is already the // proven, real answer to "break bookings down by service family." // Auto-generated narrative for Executive Summary (2026-09-06, Misty: // "should we also have a summary or narrative here also" -> confirmed via // AskUserQuestion: auto-generated from real data, not an editable // free-text field). Every sentence below is templated directly off fields // already computed elsewhere in this function (monthPace, revenuePace, // regionalDrivers, revenueBridge.reconciliation) -- no new query, no // invented commentary, and nothing here is a number that doesn't already // exist somewhere else on this same page. If a needed figure is null // (e.g. no trailing average yet, or revenue actuals unavailable), that // sentence is simply omitted rather than guessed at. function buildExecutiveNarrative({ monthPace, revenueAvailable, revenuePace, regionalDrivers, revenueBridge }) { const lines = []; const fmtPct = (n) => `${n >= 0 ? '+' : ''}${n.toFixed(0)}%`; const fmtMoney = (n) => { const abs = Math.abs(n); const val = abs >= 1000000 ? `$${(abs / 1000000).toFixed(1)}M` : `$${(abs / 1000).toFixed(0)}K`; return n < 0 ? `-${val}` : val; }; // Sales pace vs trailing average if (monthPace.pacePctOfTrailingAvg !== null) { const gap = monthPace.pacePctOfTrailingAvg - 100; lines.push(`Sales is pacing ${fmtPct(gap)} vs. its trailing average this month${monthPace.pacePctOfBudget !== null ? `, ${fmtPct(monthPace.pacePctOfBudget - 100)} vs. budget` : ''}.`); } // Revenue pace vs trailing average, only when real billed data exists if (revenueAvailable && revenuePace && revenuePace.pacePctOfTrailingAvg !== null) { const gap = revenuePace.pacePctOfTrailingAvg - 100; lines.push(`Revenue is pacing ${fmtPct(gap)} vs. its trailing average${revenuePace.pacePctOfBudget !== null ? `, ${fmtPct(revenuePace.pacePctOfBudget - 100)} vs. budget` : ''}.`); } else { lines.push(`Revenue actuals are not yet available for any region this fiscal year, so Revenue pace cannot be shown.`); } // Worst-pacing region and its named driver (regions are already sorted // furthest-below-pace first by getForecastRegionalDrivers) const worst = regionalDrivers.regions.find(r => r.pacePctOfTrailingAvg !== null && r.pacePctOfTrailingAvg < 100); if (worst) { let sentence = `${worst.regionName} is the furthest below its own Sales pace (${fmtPct(worst.pacePctOfTrailingAvg - 100)}, ${fmtMoney(worst.dollarVsAvgMonth)} vs. avg month)`; if (worst.shortfallLob) { sentence += `, driven mainly by ${worst.shortfallLob.label}`; if (worst.shortfallServiceFamily) sentence += ` (${worst.shortfallServiceFamily.serviceFamily})`; } lines.push(sentence + '.'); } // Worst-pacing region on the Revenue basis, and its named driver. // regions[].revenue (only present when revenueActualsAvailable) is the // raw computeRegionDriverForScope result, so its shortfall fields are // named shortfallLob / shortfallGroup (groupLabel), not the flattened // shortfallServiceFamily alias used for the Sales bullet above -- read // straight off revenue.shortfallGroup.groupLabel instead. Not // necessarily the same region as the Sales bullet's worst region. if (revenueAvailable) { const worstRevenue = regionalDrivers.regions .filter(r => r.revenue && r.revenue.pacePctOfTrailingAvg !== null && r.revenue.pacePctOfTrailingAvg < 100) .sort((a, b) => a.revenue.pacePctOfTrailingAvg - b.revenue.pacePctOfTrailingAvg)[0]; if (worstRevenue) { let sentence = `${worstRevenue.regionName} is the furthest below its own Revenue pace (${fmtPct(worstRevenue.revenue.pacePctOfTrailingAvg - 100)}, ${fmtMoney(worstRevenue.revenue.dollarVsAvgMonth)} vs. avg month)`; if (worstRevenue.revenue.shortfallLob) { sentence += `, driven mainly by ${worstRevenue.revenue.shortfallLob.label}`; if (worstRevenue.revenue.shortfallGroup) sentence += ` (${worstRevenue.revenue.shortfallGroup.groupLabel})`; } lines.push(sentence + '.'); } } // Revenue Bridge reconciliation bullet REMOVED (2026-09-11, Open Items // 1(a)): this sentence was a holdover from when the Revenue Bridge // chart/table lived on this same page. The Bridge display moved to // Forecast > Revenue Bridge on 2026-09-10 (see the comment on // renderExecutiveSummary's own call site, and getExecutiveSummary below // which still fetches revenueBridge for its data but no longer // surfaces this line). Floating on its own here, with no chart to // reference, the sentence no longer made sense — confirmed with Misty. // revenueBridge is still passed into this function for now (harmless, // unused) rather than threading a signature change through // getExecutiveSummary for a parameter that may find another use later. return lines; } async function getExecutiveSummary(env, fiscalYear) { const [monthPace, regionalDrivers, revenueBridge, lobBacklog, companyRevenueTrend] = await Promise.all([ getForecastMonthPace(env, fiscalYear), getForecastRegionalDrivers(env, fiscalYear), getRevenueBridge(env, fiscalYear, null), getCompanyLobBacklog(env, fiscalYear), getCompanyRevenueTrend(env, fiscalYear), ]); // Company-wide Revenue pace tile (2026-09-06, added per Misty's review // of the Executive Summary mockup: "pace tiles are heavily sales // focused, need another billing/revenue tile"). // // 2026-09-10 FIX: this used to sum the per-region Revenue figures // getForecastRegionalDrivers computes, but that function's billed_fact // query is deliberately WHERE region_id IS NOT NULL (correct for ITS // job — it can't attribute an unmapped row to a specific region), so // any billed_fact row whose raw region text never resolved through // region_raw_mappings was silently dropped from this company-wide // total, while Forecast's own "Revenue Company Blended Forecast" tile // (getForecastMonthPace, no region filter at all) kept counting it. // Misty caught this directly: two different Revenue YTD numbers on two // real screens, same underlying data ($95.20M on Forecast vs. $89.05M // here). Confirmed with Misty: company-wide totals should include // unmapped dollars, not silently drop them (per-region attribution of // those dollars is a separate, real gap — same class as the // still-unresolved "APEX" region mapping on billed_line_item_fact). // Fix: just use monthPace.revenuePace directly — it's already the // real, complete, correct company-wide Revenue blended-forecast // (unfiltered billed_fact query, full budget/trailing-avg math), no // reason to reconstruct a second, different total from a per-region // sum. revenueAvailable now reads off monthPace too, for the same // reason (regionalDrivers.revenueAvailable could theoretically differ // if literally all revenue were in unmapped rows, an edge case that no // longer matters once this reads the unfiltered source). const revenueAvailable = monthPace.revenueAvailable; const revenuePace = monthPace.revenuePace; // Sales (Bookings) by Service Family moved to Pipeline & Backlog's // Service Mix tab (2026-09-08) — this used to compute it here for // Executive Summary; getPipelineBacklogScreen now computes it in its // own fiscal-year- and region-scoped way, so it's gone from here. const narrative = buildExecutiveNarrative({ monthPace, revenueAvailable, revenuePace, regionalDrivers, revenueBridge }); // Book-to-Bill Ratio, Company Backlog, Top Performing Region (2026-09-08, // McKinsey-pass gaps confirmed with Misty, §97/§98). All three reuse data // this function or getForecastRegionalDrivers already computed elsewhere // in the app, no new fact-table queries duplicated except the one // company-wide backlog sum below. // // Book-to-Bill: Sales month-blended / Revenue month-blended, this month. // Null (not zero) when Revenue isn't available yet or its blended figure // is $0, same "explicit pending state, never a fake number" rule used // throughout this app. const bookToBillRatioPct = (revenueAvailable && revenuePace && revenuePace.currentMonthBlended > 0) ? (monthPace.currentMonth.blended / revenuePace.currentMonthBlended) * 100 : null; // Company Backlog + YTD Revenue vs. Revenue Budget (2026-09-09/10, per // Misty's direct requests — see getCompanyBacklogCurrentYear's own // comment block for the full history). Pulled into that shared helper // (2026-09-10) so Growth Target's "vs. YTD Actual + Backlog" / "vs. // Revenue Budget" comparisons read the exact same real figures as this // tile, instead of a second parallel derivation. const companyBacklogCurrentYear = await getCompanyBacklogCurrentYear(env, fiscalYear, revenueAvailable, revenuePace); const companyBacklog = companyBacklogCurrentYear.backlogTotal; // Book-to-Bill Ratio, YTD (2026-09-10, per Misty's direct request: "for // book to bill ratio can you give me a YTD percentage as well... that's // on exec summary"). Same Sales / Revenue relationship as the current- // month figure above, but full-year-to-date instead of one month. // Sales YTD reuses monthPace's own real actuals-to-date sum (same real // figures Growth Target's Sales YTD reference note uses); Revenue YTD // reuses companyBacklogCurrentYear.revenueYtdActual, computed just // above, rather than a third independent derivation of the same real // number. Null, not a fake 0%, when either side has no real YTD yet. const salesYtdActual = (monthPace.priorActualsSum || 0) + (monthPace.currentMonth?.actualsToDate || 0); const bookToBillRatioYtdPct = (companyBacklogCurrentYear.revenueYtdActual !== null && companyBacklogCurrentYear.revenueYtdActual > 0) ? (salesYtdActual / companyBacklogCurrentYear.revenueYtdActual) * 100 : null; // Top Performing Region: Sales and Revenue tracked separately since the // leading region can differ (it does today) — each is the region with // the highest pacePctOfBudget on that basis. Revenue only considers // regions with revenueActualsAvailable, matching how Revenue is treated // everywhere else in this app. let topPerformingRegionSales = null; for (const r of regionalDrivers.regions) { if (r.pacePctOfBudget === null || r.pacePctOfBudget === undefined) continue; if (!topPerformingRegionSales || r.pacePctOfBudget > topPerformingRegionSales.pacePctOfBudget) { topPerformingRegionSales = { regionId: r.regionId, regionName: r.regionName, pacePctOfBudget: r.pacePctOfBudget }; } } let topPerformingRegionRevenue = null; for (const r of regionalDrivers.regions) { if (!r.revenueActualsAvailable || !r.revenue || r.revenue.pacePctOfBudget === null || r.revenue.pacePctOfBudget === undefined) continue; if (!topPerformingRegionRevenue || r.revenue.pacePctOfBudget > topPerformingRegionRevenue.pacePctOfBudget) { topPerformingRegionRevenue = { regionId: r.regionId, regionName: r.regionName, pacePctOfBudget: r.revenue.pacePctOfBudget }; } } // Top Performing Region, YTD (2026-09-09, per Misty's direct request: // "MTD to be obvious but I'd like to see YTD too"). Basis confirmed with // Misty: YTD actual vs. YTD budget-to-date (budget prorated through the // months and business days elapsed so far), the same pacePctOfYtdBudget // computeRegionDriverForScope now returns for both Sales and Revenue. // Mirrors the MTD loops above exactly, just reading the YTD field. let topPerformingRegionSalesYtd = null; for (const r of regionalDrivers.regions) { if (r.pacePctOfYtdBudget === null || r.pacePctOfYtdBudget === undefined) continue; if (!topPerformingRegionSalesYtd || r.pacePctOfYtdBudget > topPerformingRegionSalesYtd.pacePctOfYtdBudget) { topPerformingRegionSalesYtd = { regionId: r.regionId, regionName: r.regionName, pacePctOfYtdBudget: r.pacePctOfYtdBudget }; } } let topPerformingRegionRevenueYtd = null; for (const r of regionalDrivers.regions) { if (!r.revenueActualsAvailable || !r.revenue || r.revenue.pacePctOfYtdBudget === null || r.revenue.pacePctOfYtdBudget === undefined) continue; if (!topPerformingRegionRevenueYtd || r.revenue.pacePctOfYtdBudget > topPerformingRegionRevenueYtd.pacePctOfYtdBudget) { topPerformingRegionRevenueYtd = { regionId: r.regionId, regionName: r.regionName, pacePctOfYtdBudget: r.revenue.pacePctOfYtdBudget }; } } // Top Sales Performers / Top Billers, company-wide (2026-09-10, per // Misty's direct request, following the same real leaderboard cards // built for the Regions mockup — same ranking logic, region filter // just dropped). Top 5 each, confirmed with Misty. const [topSalesPerformers, topBillers] = await Promise.all([ getTopSalesPerformers(env, fiscalYear, { regionId: null, limit: 5 }), getTopBillers(env, fiscalYear, { regionId: null, limit: 5 }), ]); return { fiscalYear, monthPace, revenueAvailable, revenuePace, regionalDrivers, revenueBridge, narrative, bookToBillRatioPct, bookToBillRatioYtdPct, companyBacklog, companyBacklogCurrentYear, topPerformingRegionSales, topPerformingRegionRevenue, topPerformingRegionSalesYtd, topPerformingRegionRevenueYtd, lobBacklog, topSalesPerformers, topBillers, companyRevenueTrend, }; } // Top Sales Performers (2026-09-10): real, from bookings_fact. // proposal_issued_by (populated, per-deal sales attribution — see §99's // grounding, confirmed unused anywhere in PULSE before this). Ranked by // SUM(total_price), this fiscal year, optionally scoped to one region // (the Regions mockup's own card); regionId: null is the company-wide // Executive Summary case. Blank/null proposal_issued_by rows are // excluded (can't attribute them to a person), same "explicit gap, don't // fake it" convention as everywhere else, rather than showing a // misleading "(blank)" leaderboard row. // Region attribution (2026-09-11, Open Items 1(d)): Misty wants a region // subline next to each leaderboard name. Basis confirmed with Misty: // each person's single largest-dollar region this fiscal year (most // people work one region in practice, so this reads the same as "their // region"; it's a real judgment call only for someone with meaningful // activity split across regions, and largest-dollar is the least // arbitrary tiebreak). One extra grouped query per leaderboard, same // conditions/params as the main ranking query, then reduced client-side // (in this function) to each name's top region — simpler than a window // function and D1's SQL surface doesn't reliably support one. async function loadTopRegionByName(env, table, nameCol, amountCol, conditions, params) { const rows = (await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT ${nameCol} AS name, region_id, SUM(${amountCol}) AS total FROM ${table} WHERE ${conditions.join(' AND ')} AND region_id IS NOT NULL GROUP BY ${nameCol}, region_id` ).bind(...params).all()).results || []; const best = new Map(); // name -> { regionId, total } for (const r of rows) { const cur = best.get(r.name); if (!cur || (r.total || 0) > cur.total) best.set(r.name, { regionId: r.region_id, total: r.total || 0 }); } return best; } async function getTopSalesPerformers(env, fiscalYear, { regionId = null, limit = 5 } = {}) { const conditions = [`${AWARDED_YEAR_SQL} = ?`, `awarded_date IS NOT NULL`, `awarded_date != ''`, `proposal_issued_by IS NOT NULL`, `proposal_issued_by != ''`]; const params = [fiscalYear]; if (regionId) { conditions.push(`region_id = ?`); params.push(regionId); } const rows = (await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT proposal_issued_by, SUM(total_price) AS total, COUNT(*) AS dealCount FROM bookings_fact WHERE ${conditions.join(' AND ')} GROUP BY proposal_issued_by ORDER BY total DESC LIMIT ?` ).bind(...params, limit).all()).results || []; const grandTotalRow = await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT SUM(total_price) AS total FROM bookings_fact WHERE ${conditions.join(' AND ')}` ).bind(...params).first(); const grandTotal = (grandTotalRow && grandTotalRow.total) || 0; // Region subline (1(d)): skip the extra query/lookup entirely when // already scoped to one region — the region is implied and would be // redundant to repeat on every row. let topRegionByName = new Map(), regionNamesById = new Map(); if (!regionId && rows.length > 0) { topRegionByName = await loadTopRegionByName(env, 'bookings_fact', 'proposal_issued_by', 'total_price', conditions, params); const regions = await listRegions(env); regionNamesById = new Map(regions.map(r => [r.id, r.name])); } return { available: rows.length > 0, performers: rows.map(r => ({ name: r.proposal_issued_by, total: r.total || 0, dealCount: r.dealCount || 0, pctOfTotal: grandTotal > 0 ? ((r.total || 0) / grandTotal) * 100 : null, regionName: topRegionByName.has(r.proposal_issued_by) ? (regionNamesById.get(topRegionByName.get(r.proposal_issued_by).regionId) || null) : null, })), }; } // Top Billers (2026-09-10): real, from billed_line_item_fact. // employee_name — the new real per-employee billing source (§99's // billing-side gap, closed by this table; billed_fact itself still has // no individual field at all). 2026-forward only, so a fiscal year // before that returns available: false, same explicit-pending-state // convention as Revenue before its first billed_fact upload, never a // fake zero. Ranked by SUM(amount), this fiscal year, optionally scoped // to one region. async function getTopBillers(env, fiscalYear, { regionId = null, limit = 5 } = {}) { const conditions = [`fiscal_year = ?`, `employee_name IS NOT NULL`, `employee_name != ''`]; const params = [fiscalYear]; if (regionId) { conditions.push(`region_id = ?`); params.push(regionId); } const rows = (await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT employee_name, SUM(amount) AS total, COUNT(*) AS lineCount FROM billed_line_item_fact WHERE ${conditions.join(' AND ')} GROUP BY employee_name ORDER BY total DESC LIMIT ?` ).bind(...params, limit).all()).results || []; const grandTotalRow = await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT SUM(amount) AS total FROM billed_line_item_fact WHERE ${conditions.join(' AND ')}` ).bind(...params).first(); const grandTotal = (grandTotalRow && grandTotalRow.total) || 0; // Region subline (1(e)), same basis/approach as Top Sales Performers // above: each person's largest-dollar region this fiscal year. let topRegionByName = new Map(), regionNamesById = new Map(); if (!regionId && rows.length > 0) { topRegionByName = await loadTopRegionByName(env, 'billed_line_item_fact', 'employee_name', 'amount', conditions, params); const regions = await listRegions(env); regionNamesById = new Map(regions.map(r => [r.id, r.name])); } return { available: rows.length > 0, billers: rows.map(r => ({ name: r.employee_name, total: r.total || 0, lineCount: r.lineCount || 0, pctOfTotal: grandTotal > 0 ? ((r.total || 0) / grandTotal) * 100 : null, regionName: topRegionByName.has(r.employee_name) ? (regionNamesById.get(topRegionByName.get(r.employee_name).regionId) || null) : null, })), }; } // ---- Account Mapping review (2026-09-05) ------------------------------- // // bookings_fact.parent_company (a real, coarse "parent company" grain, // ~221 distinct values) and backlog_fact.parent_customer_name (a much // finer "specific building/property/management-company" grain, ~2,184 // distinct values) are both free-text columns sourced straight from raw // Salesforce export headers, neither has a customer ID. Confirmed // directly against live data: this is a structural grain mismatch, not // just inconsistent spelling (e.g. bookings' "Cushman & Wakefield" vs. // backlog's "Cushman & Wakefield (500 New Jersey)" and "Cushman & // Wakefield, Inc.-World Headquarters" as separate, more specific rows). // // A naive automatic join was tested against real data and rejected: a // substring/first-two-words heuristic wrongly matched "Partners" to 3 // unrelated accounts and wrongly matched "Dominium, Inc." to "Boca Bayou // Condominiums". So this follows the same pattern as region_raw_mappings: // a real, admin-curated mapping table (account_mappings), with any // algorithmic match shown only as a suggestion the admin must explicitly // confirm, never auto-applied. // // Scope, confirmed with Misty from a real count against live data: only // the bookings_fact.parent_company values with zero normalized match in // backlog_fact.parent_customer_name need review here (89 accounts as of // the 2026-09-05 analysis). backlog names with no corresponding bookings // account are expected and out of scope (many are one-off buildings or // condo associations that may never appear in bookings). // Lowercases, strips periods/commas, and strips common legal-entity // suffixes so "Cushman & Wakefield, Inc." and "cushman & wakefield inc" // normalize the same. Used only to detect matches/suggestions, never // written anywhere, real account_mappings rows always store the actual // display names. const ACCOUNT_SUFFIX_RE = /\b(inc|llc|llp|lp|corp|corporation|co|ltd)\b\.?/g; function normalizeAccountName(name) { if (!name) return ''; return String(name) .toLowerCase() .replace(/[.,]/g, '') .replace(ACCOUNT_SUFFIX_RE, '') .replace(/\s+/g, ' ') .trim(); } // A backlog name is a plausible suggestion for a bookings account when, // after normalizing both, one contains the other or they share the same // first two words. Deliberately noisy (confirmed against real data to // produce false positives like "Partners" matching 3 unrelated accounts), // so this is surfaced as a suggestion only, the admin decides. function findAccountSuggestions(bookingsName, backlogNames) { const norm = normalizeAccountName(bookingsName); if (!norm) return []; const normWords = norm.split(' '); const firstTwo = normWords.slice(0, 2).join(' '); const suggestions = []; for (const backlogName of backlogNames) { const backlogNorm = normalizeAccountName(backlogName); if (!backlogNorm) continue; if (backlogNorm === norm) continue; // would already be an exact match, not unmatched const contains = backlogNorm.includes(norm) || norm.includes(backlogNorm); const sharesPrefix = firstTwo.length > 2 && backlogNorm.startsWith(firstTwo); if (contains || sharesPrefix) suggestions.push(backlogName); } return suggestions; } // Review-queue threshold (continuity brief §70, confirmed 2026-09-06): // scopes ONLY which accounts an admin is asked to manually review below. // Never read as, or used as, a dollar cutoff anywhere else in the app — // every account's bookings/backlog dollars count toward every total // shown elsewhere, reviewed or not. Chosen from a real distribution // analysis of the 89 unmatched accounts' lifetime bookings totals // ($522,806 across all 89, from $67,144.60 down to $286): $5,000+ // surfaces 32 of 89 (36%) for manual review. const ACCOUNT_MAPPING_REVIEW_THRESHOLD = 5000; // The review list: every bookings_fact.parent_company with zero // normalized match in backlog_fact.parent_customer_name, split into the // above-threshold queue (with fuzzy suggestions, confirmed mappings, and // no-match review status) and the below-threshold list (name + value // only, never queued for review). Both distinct-name lists are small // (low thousands of rows at most) and re-derived on every call rather // than cached, this endpoint is an occasional admin-review tool, not a // hot path. async function getAccountMappingReview(env) { const bookingsRows = (await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT DISTINCT parent_company FROM bookings_fact WHERE parent_company IS NOT NULL AND parent_company != ''` ).all()).results || []; const backlogRows = (await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT DISTINCT parent_customer_name FROM backlog_fact WHERE parent_customer_name IS NOT NULL AND parent_customer_name != ''` ).all()).results || []; const mappingRows = (await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT raw_backlog_name, canonical_account_name FROM account_mappings` ).all()).results || []; const reviewRows = (await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT bookings_account_name, status FROM account_mapping_reviews` ).all()).results || []; // Lifetime bookings $ per parent_company, all ~221 distinct values — // this is display/threshold-scoping data only, it never filters or // affects any dollar total shown elsewhere in the app (brief §70). const totalsRows = (await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT parent_company, SUM(total_price) AS total FROM bookings_fact WHERE parent_company IS NOT NULL AND parent_company != '' GROUP BY parent_company` ).all()).results || []; const backlogNames = backlogRows.map(r => r.parent_customer_name); const backlogNormSet = new Set(backlogNames.map(normalizeAccountName)); const mappingByCanonical = new Map(); // canonical_account_name -> [raw_backlog_name, ...] for (const m of mappingRows) { if (!mappingByCanonical.has(m.canonical_account_name)) mappingByCanonical.set(m.canonical_account_name, []); mappingByCanonical.get(m.canonical_account_name).push(m.raw_backlog_name); } const noMatchSet = new Set(reviewRows.filter(r => r.status === 'no_match').map(r => r.bookings_account_name)); const valueByName = new Map(totalsRows.map(r => [r.parent_company, r.total || 0])); const unmatched = bookingsRows .map(r => r.parent_company) .filter(name => !backlogNormSet.has(normalizeAccountName(name))) .map(name => ({ name, value: valueByName.get(name) || 0 })) .sort((a, b) => b.value - a.value); const aboveThreshold = unmatched.filter(a => a.value >= ACCOUNT_MAPPING_REVIEW_THRESHOLD); const belowThreshold = unmatched.filter(a => a.value < ACCOUNT_MAPPING_REVIEW_THRESHOLD); const accounts = aboveThreshold.map(({ name, value }) => ({ bookingsName: name, value, suggestions: findAccountSuggestions(name, backlogNames), confirmedMappings: mappingByCanonical.get(name) || [], noMatch: noMatchSet.has(name), })); const totalValue = unmatched.reduce((s, a) => s + a.value, 0); const aboveThresholdValue = aboveThreshold.reduce((s, a) => s + a.value, 0); return { accounts, belowThreshold: belowThreshold.map(({ name, value }) => ({ bookingsName: name, value })), threshold: ACCOUNT_MAPPING_REVIEW_THRESHOLD, totalUnmatched: unmatched.length, totalAboveThreshold: aboveThreshold.length, totalBelowThreshold: belowThreshold.length, totalValue, aboveThresholdValue, totalBacklogNames: backlogNames.length, }; } // Records that an at-or-above-threshold account was deliberately // reviewed and found to have no Backlog match — distinct from an // account that simply hasn't been looked at yet (brief §70 point 3). // Never affects any dollar total; purely a review-workflow status. async function markAccountNoMatch(env, bookingsAccountName, actorUserId) { const name = (bookingsAccountName || '').trim(); if (!name) return { error: 'bookingsAccountName is required.', status: 400 }; await env.DB.prepare( `INSERT INTO account_mapping_reviews (bookings_account_name, status, reviewed_by, reviewed_at) VALUES (?, 'no_match', ?, strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%fZ','now')) ON CONFLICT(bookings_account_name) DO UPDATE SET status = 'no_match', reviewed_by = excluded.reviewed_by, reviewed_at = excluded.reviewed_at` ).bind(name, actorUserId).run(); return { ok: true }; } // Undoes a no-match marking (the admin realizes the account wasn't // actually reviewed, or wants another look) — returns it to "never // reviewed" rather than leaving a stale record behind. async function clearAccountNoMatch(env, bookingsAccountName) { const name = (bookingsAccountName || '').trim(); if (!name) return { error: 'bookingsAccountName is required.', status: 400 }; await env.DB.prepare(`DELETE FROM account_mapping_reviews WHERE bookings_account_name = ?`).bind(name).run(); return { ok: true }; } // Free-text search across all backlog_fact.parent_customer_name values, // for the admin to find a match the fuzzy pass missed. Plain SQL LIKE, // this table is small enough that a full scan per search is fine. async function searchBacklogAccountNames(env, query) { const q = (query || '').trim(); if (!q) return []; const rows = (await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT DISTINCT parent_customer_name FROM backlog_fact WHERE parent_customer_name LIKE ? ESCAPE '\\' ORDER BY parent_customer_name ASC LIMIT 50` ).bind('%' + q.replace(/[%_\\]/g, c => '\\' + c) + '%').all()).results || []; return rows.map(r => r.parent_customer_name); } // Saves confirmed account-mapping rows in one batch, all-or-nothing. // Each mapping ties one or more raw backlog names to a single canonical // account name (matching bookings_fact.parent_company's grain). Re-saving // a raw_backlog_name updates its canonical_account_name (ON CONFLICT), // so the admin can correct a mapping later without a separate delete step. // Also clears any "no match found" review record for an account the // moment a real mapping is confirmed for it (2026-09-06, brief §70) — // mapped and no_match are mutually exclusive account states, so // confirming a match is itself the undo for an earlier no-match call. async function saveAccountMappings(env, mappings, actorUserId) { if (!Array.isArray(mappings) || mappings.length === 0) { return { error: 'At least one mapping is required.', status: 400 }; } for (const m of mappings) { if (!m || !m.rawBacklogName || !m.canonicalAccountName) { return { error: 'Each mapping needs rawBacklogName and canonicalAccountName.', status: 400 }; } } const now = new Date().toISOString(); const stmts = mappings.map(m => env.DB.prepare( `INSERT INTO account_mappings (raw_backlog_name, canonical_account_name, updated_by, updated_at) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?) ON CONFLICT(raw_backlog_name) DO UPDATE SET canonical_account_name = excluded.canonical_account_name, updated_by = excluded.updated_by, updated_at = excluded.updated_at` ).bind(m.rawBacklogName, m.canonicalAccountName, actorUserId, now)); const canonicalNames = [...new Set(mappings.map(m => m.canonicalAccountName))]; for (const name of canonicalNames) { stmts.push(env.DB.prepare(`DELETE FROM account_mapping_reviews WHERE bookings_account_name = ?`).bind(name)); } await env.DB.batch(stmts); return { ok: true, saved: mappings.length }; } // ---- Coverage upload (2026-09-06) -------------------------------------- // // Two independent roster sources, each replacing only its own rows on // reupload (source_file is the scope key, same shape as billed_fact's // fiscal_year scoping). Names are never received here at all: the // frontend parser (SheetJS, same as Billed Fact) strips the name columns // before the row ever leaves the browser, this Worker code never even // sees a header called "Legal First Name". Region is resolved from state // via state_region_map, the same table the Geography screen edits and // city_market_tiers already joins through for the Market Tier count. const VALID_COVERAGE_SOURCES = new Set(['consultants', 'inspectors']); // Currently unused by getGrowthOpportunityMarkets (redesigned 2026-09-06 // to rank every Tier 1/2 city by distance rather than apply a pass/fail // threshold), kept as a real, already-confirmed constant in case a // future view wants a "within typical service radius" indicator. const COVERAGE_RADIUS_MILES = 50; // Consultants field roles: Consultant, Area Manager count; Senior Vice // President, Sales, Regional Vice President, Engineer are stored but // excluded. Inspectors: Field Examiner counts; Sales does not. Matched // case-sensitively against the real Job Function Description values // confirmed directly from both source files. const COVERAGE_COUNTING_ROLES = new Set(['Consultant', 'Area Manager', 'Field Examiner']); // coverage_roster.id is INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT (matching // city_market_tiers' own convention, since these rows have no natural // external key the way an upload_batch id or account name does), so it // is never supplied here, unlike account_mapping_reviews/billed_fact // which use randomToken for a TEXT PRIMARY KEY. Passing a hex id into an // AUTOINCREMENT column is exactly what caused the real 500 on first // upload, fixed 2026-09-06 by leaving id out of every insert below. function mapCoverageRow(row, regionMap) { const role = (row['role'] || '').trim(); const city = (row['city'] || '').trim(); const state = (row['state'] || '').trim(); if (!role || !city || !state) return null; return { source_file: row['sourceFile'], role, department_raw: (row['departmentRaw'] || '').trim() || null, city, state, region_id: regionMap.get(state) || null, counts_toward_coverage: COVERAGE_COUNTING_ROLES.has(role) ? 1 : 0, }; } const COVERAGE_ROSTER_COLUMNS = ['upload_batch_id', 'source_file', 'role', 'department_raw', 'city', 'state', 'region_id', 'counts_toward_coverage']; // Real state_region_map lookup (full state name -> region_id), distinct // from loadRegionMap/resolveRegionId above which resolve raw Salesforce // region text through region_raw_mappings, a different table that // doesn't apply to roster rows at all. async function loadStateRegionMap(env) { const rows = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT state, region_id FROM state_region_map').all(); const map = new Map(); for (const r of (rows.results || [])) map.set(r.state, r.region_id); return map; } // Replaces (not appends) this source_file's coverage_roster rows with a // freshly-uploaded set, mirroring replaceBilledFactForYear's delete+ // insert-in-one-batch shape but scoped by source_file instead of // fiscal_year (coverage has no year dimension, it's always "the current // roster"). async function replaceCoverageRoster(env, sourceFile, uploadBatchId, rows) { if (!VALID_COVERAGE_SOURCES.has(sourceFile)) return { error: 'Invalid source file.', status: 400 }; if (!Array.isArray(rows) || rows.length === 0) return { error: 'No rows provided.', status: 400 }; const stateRegionMap = await loadStateRegionMap(env); const mapped = rows .map(r => mapCoverageRow({ ...r, sourceFile }, stateRegionMap)) .filter(Boolean); if (mapped.length === 0) { return { error: 'No usable rows found (each row needs a role, city, and state).', status: 400 }; } const placeholders = '(' + COVERAGE_ROSTER_COLUMNS.map(() => '?').join(',') + ')'; const insertSql = `INSERT INTO coverage_roster (${COVERAGE_ROSTER_COLUMNS.join(',')}) VALUES ${placeholders}`; const deleteStmt = env.DB.prepare('DELETE FROM coverage_roster WHERE source_file = ?').bind(sourceFile); let inserted = 0; for (let i = 0; i < mapped.length; i += BATCH_INSERT_SIZE) { const slice = mapped.slice(i, i + BATCH_INSERT_SIZE).map(row => ({ upload_batch_id: uploadBatchId, ...row })); const stmts = slice.map(row => env.DB.prepare(insertSql).bind(...COVERAGE_ROSTER_COLUMNS.map(c => row[c] ?? null))); if (i === 0) { await env.DB.batch([deleteStmt, ...stmts]); } else { await env.DB.batch(stmts); } inserted += slice.length; } await env.DB.prepare('UPDATE upload_batches SET row_count = ? WHERE id = ?').bind(inserted, uploadBatchId).run(); const unmapped = [...new Set(mapped.filter(r => !r.region_id).map(r => r.state))]; return { ok: true, rowsInserted: inserted, unmappedStates: unmapped }; } // Haversine great-circle distance in miles between two lat/lng points. function haversineMiles(lat1, lng1, lat2, lng2) { const R = 3958.8; // Earth radius in miles const toRad = d => (d * Math.PI) / 180; const dLat = toRad(lat2 - lat1); const dLng = toRad(lng2 - lng1); const a = Math.sin(dLat / 2) ** 2 + Math.cos(toRad(lat1)) * Math.cos(toRad(lat2)) * Math.sin(dLng / 2) ** 2; return 2 * R * Math.asin(Math.sqrt(a)); } // Redesigned 2026-09-06, replacing the original pass/fail "under-covered" // list. Per Misty's direct correction: the point isn't a 50-mile // threshold check, it's a business-development view — every Tier 1/2 // market in the region, ranked by how far the nearest team member is, // so the farthest (least-served) markets surface first regardless of // whether they technically clear some radius. Real bookings/backlog // have no city or state column at all (bookings_fact/backlog_fact are // region-scoped only), so this can't show real per-city revenue; it // shows the region's real bookings total as context instead and leaves // per-city revenue for later, if a real project-location field // (job-site city, not just region) becomes available. // // Keeps the company-wide roster-distance fix from the earlier bug // (physical proximity doesn't respect region boundaries): the roster // side is never filtered by region, only the Tier-city side is. async function getGrowthOpportunityMarkets(env, regionId) { const tierCities = (await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT cmt.state, cmt.city, cmt.tier, cg.lat, cg.lng, cmt.priority_score, cmt.recommended_priority, cmt.estimated_vt_installed_base, cmt.major_market_verticals FROM city_market_tiers cmt JOIN state_region_map srm ON srm.state = cmt.state LEFT JOIN city_geocodes cg ON cg.state = cmt.state AND cg.city = cmt.city WHERE srm.region_id = ? AND cmt.tier IN ('tier_1', 'tier_2')` ).bind(regionId).all()).results || []; const rosterPoints = (await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT DISTINCT cr.city, cr.state, cr.role, cr.source_file, cg.lat, cg.lng FROM coverage_roster cr JOIN city_geocodes cg ON cg.state = cr.state AND cg.city = cr.city WHERE cr.counts_toward_coverage = 1` ).all()).results || []; const markets = []; const geocodeMissing = []; for (const tc of tierCities) { if (tc.lat == null || tc.lng == null) { geocodeMissing.push(`${tc.city}, ${tc.state}`); continue; } let nearestMiles = Infinity; let nearestPoint = null; for (const rp of rosterPoints) { const dist = haversineMiles(tc.lat, tc.lng, rp.lat, rp.lng); if (dist < nearestMiles) { nearestMiles = dist; nearestPoint = rp; } } markets.push({ city: tc.city, state: tc.state, tier: tc.tier, nearestMiles: Number.isFinite(nearestMiles) ? Math.round(nearestMiles) : null, nearestCity: nearestPoint ? nearestPoint.city : null, nearestRole: nearestPoint ? nearestPoint.role : null, nearestSourceFile: nearestPoint ? nearestPoint.source_file : null, // Market Prioritization scoring (2026-09-12), context only per // Misty's direct decision — never used to re-rank this list, never // written back to cmt.tier. Null for any city not yet scored // (only city_market_tiers' real 119 rows carry this data). priorityScore: tc.priority_score ?? null, recommendedPriority: tc.recommended_priority ?? null, estimatedVtInstalledBase: tc.estimated_vt_installed_base ?? null, majorMarketVerticals: tc.major_market_verticals ?? null, }); } // Farthest (least-served) first; a city with nobody geocoded at all // has no distance to sort by, so those fall last rather than first. markets.sort((a, b) => (b.nearestMiles ?? -1) - (a.nearestMiles ?? -1)); return { markets, geocodeMissing }; } // Curates getGrowthOpportunityMarkets' full farthest-first list down to a // Top 5, per Open Items 2(d) (2026-09-11): "This should be a Top 5 List // of growth opportunity markets... allow me to dismiss a city, and // recommend an automatic replacement." Dismissed city+region pairs live // in market_dismissals (new table, see schema note at call site) so a // dismissal persists across sessions; the next-ranked market not already // dismissed auto-fills the 5th slot, same ranking logic as before. // // Also attaches, per market: (1) an approximate "nearby backlog" dollar // figure, and (2) a plain-language reason it's on the list. Both use // only real, already-stored data — no invented taxonomy: // // - The $ figure parses backlog_fact.project_name (this region only) // for a confident, whole-word, case-insensitive match against the // market's city name, and sums contract_balance across every // matching row. bookings_fact/backlog_fact have no city/lat-long // column of their own (confirmed against the real schema), but real // Opportunity/Project Name values do embed the job-site city as free // text (e.g. "...360 Nueces Street, Austin-"), per the documented // naming convention in the sales continuity brief. A row with no // confident match is skipped silently rather than guessed at, so // this is a floor on real nearby business, not a precise total, and // is labeled "approximate" wherever it's shown. // - The reason line uses only market tier + distance rank, today's // actual ranking inputs, stated plainly rather than inventing a // scoring rationale PULSE has no data to back (market size, VT // install base, equipment age, and vertical mix were all confirmed // absent from the schema and are explicitly out of scope here, held // as their own future item per Misty's direct answer, 2026-09-11). async function getTopGrowthOpportunityMarkets(env, regionId, fiscalYear) { const { markets: allMarkets, geocodeMissing } = await getGrowthOpportunityMarkets(env, regionId); const dismissedRows = (await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT city, state FROM market_dismissals WHERE region_id = ?` ).bind(regionId).all()).results || []; const dismissedSet = new Set(dismissedRows.map(r => `${r.city}|${r.state}`)); const eligible = allMarkets.filter(m => !dismissedSet.has(`${m.city}|${m.state}`)); const top5 = eligible.slice(0, 5); // Real backlog rows for this region only, parsed once and matched // per-market in JS (D1/SQLite has no word-boundary regex operator // available here, so LIKE alone risks a false match like "Austin" // inside "Austintown"; a JS \b regex per city avoids that). const backlogRows = (await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT project_name, contract_balance FROM backlog_fact WHERE region_id = ? AND contract_balance IS NOT NULL AND project_name IS NOT NULL` ).bind(regionId).all()).results || []; const enriched = top5.map((m, idx) => { const cityRe = new RegExp(`\\b${m.city.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&')}\\b`, 'i'); let nearbyBacklog = null; let matchCount = 0; for (const row of backlogRows) { if (cityRe.test(row.project_name)) { nearbyBacklog = (nearbyBacklog || 0) + (row.contract_balance || 0); matchCount++; } } const tierLabel = m.tier === 'tier_1' ? 'Tier 1' : m.tier === 'tier_2' ? 'Tier 2' : 'Tier 3'; const rankWord = ['1st', '2nd', '3rd', '4th', '5th'][idx] || `${idx + 1}th`; let whyOnList = `${tierLabel} market, ${rankWord}-farthest from coverage in this region`; // Market Prioritization enrichment (2026-09-12): adds real, sourced // context to the same sentence when this city has been scored — the // ranking itself (distance-first) is untouched, per Misty's direct // decision to keep score as context, not a re-ranking input. if (m.recommendedPriority != null && m.priorityScore != null) { const unitsPhrase = m.estimatedVtInstalledBase != null ? `, modeled ~${Math.round(m.estimatedVtInstalledBase).toLocaleString('en-US')} VT units` : ''; const verticalsPhrase = m.majorMarketVerticals ? `, led by ${m.majorMarketVerticals.split(';').map(v => v.trim()).slice(0, 2).join(' and ')}` : ''; whyOnList += `, ${m.recommendedPriority} (score ${Math.round(m.priorityScore * 10) / 10})${unitsPhrase}${verticalsPhrase}`; } return { ...m, rank: idx + 1, nearbyBacklogApprox: nearbyBacklog !== null ? Math.round(nearbyBacklog * 100) / 100 : null, nearbyBacklogMatchCount: matchCount, whyOnList, }; }); return { markets: enriched, dismissedCount: dismissedSet.size, totalEligible: eligible.length, geocodeMissing, }; } async function dismissGrowthOpportunityMarket(env, regionId, city, state, actorUserId) { await env.DB.prepare( `INSERT INTO market_dismissals (id, region_id, city, state, dismissed_at, dismissed_by) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?) ON CONFLICT(region_id, city, state) DO NOTHING` ).bind(randomToken(16), regionId, city, state, new Date().toISOString(), actorUserId).run(); return getTopGrowthOpportunityMarkets(env, regionId); } async function restoreGrowthOpportunityMarket(env, regionId, city, state) { await env.DB.prepare( `DELETE FROM market_dismissals WHERE region_id = ? AND city = ? AND state = ?` ).bind(regionId, city, state).run(); return getTopGrowthOpportunityMarkets(env, regionId); } // Backs the mockup's "Show Dismissed" link (2026-09-11): named cities so // a dismissed market can be picked back out by name, not just a count. async function listDismissedGrowthOpportunityMarkets(env, regionId) { const rows = (await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT city, state, dismissed_at FROM market_dismissals WHERE region_id = ? ORDER BY dismissed_at DESC` ).bind(regionId).all()).results || []; return rows; } // ---- Market Prioritization detail (2026-09-12) ------------------------- // // Batch 4 of the approved build sequence (Market Prioritization // integration, see PULSE-market-prioritization-scoping.md).
